ShelkeVinayakumarVinodYSShrishtiKVarshaTSamirkantANidhiSArchanaPAmitJGauravSDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Safdarjung Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: kumarvinaya1993@gmail.com
Introduction: Acute appendicitis is the most common acute abdominal surgical condition. Surgical management has been the mainstay of treatment over the years; however, nonoperative management (NOMA) has gained the momentum in recent years.
Aim: To compare the outcomes in the children undergoing NOMA of noncomplicated appendicitis versus those undergoing upfront surgery.
Materials and Methods: Data of all children admitted for acute appendicitis (complicated/uncomplicated) over a period of 5 years, namely diagnosis, mode of management, i.e., surgical or conservative, postoperative course/complications, and duration of stay were retrospectively reviewed.
Results: A total of 311 children were admitted for acute appendicitis over 5 years June 2017–June 2022. Uncomplicated appendicitis was seen in 253 (81.3%) patients, 221 of them underwent NOMA while 32 were operated for persisting symptoms and clinical illness. Complicated appendicitis was found in 58 (18.7%) cases which were further operated and were excluded. Out of 32 surgically managed cases, 22 had a history of prolonged conservative management elsewhere while 10 patients did not show any clinical relief even after a conservative management of a mean 4 days. The mean duration of stay in the NOMA group was 2.3 days as compared to a longer mean duration of 8 days in the operative group. The mean duration to full feeds was longer in the operative group (6 days) as compared to the NOMA group (2 days). Recurrent appendicitis leading to surgical management was seen in 28/253 (11.1%) patients.
Conclusion: NOMA lessens the operative costs and postoperative morbidity, hospital stay, and its complications. A conservative management must be the first line of treatment for uncomplicated appendicitis.
ABSTRACT NO.: 207: Outcome of pneumatic reductions in intussusception and factors associated for its failure: Category: GIT
SaravagolVidya MBahubaliGDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College and Research Institute, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: vidya095@gmail.com
Aim: To assess the outcome of pneumatic reduction in intussusception and evaluate the factors associated for its failure.
Materials and Methods: A retrospective study was conducted in the tertiary care hospital. We collected data from the electronic medical records from 2013 to May 2022 in BMCRI. The data collected included demographic parameters (age, gender, and weight), presentation (duration of symptoms, previous history of intussusception, pain abdomen, abdominal distension, vomiting, bleeding per rectum, palpable mass, and peritonitis), ultrasonography findings, and the method of intervention (nonoperative reduction or surgery). A total of 180 patients were included in the study. The descriptive variables were expressed as number and percentage, whereas the continuous data were expressed as mean ± standard deviation or median (range).
Results: A total of 122 (67%) children underwent pneumatic reduction. 100 (86%) children had successful pneumatic reduction, 20 (14%) had to undergo surgery due to failed reduction, 9 child with gangrene and 5 child had lead point, 2 child had perforation following pneumatic reduction, 112 (69%) children who presented at or after 48 h of onset of symptoms, they had higher incidence of abdominal distension.
Conclusion: Nonoperative reduction (pneumatic) is the treatment of choice for intussusception with good outcome. The factors associated with failed pneumatic reduction include late presentation, i.e., at or after 48 h of onset of symptoms and the presence of abdominal distension at presentation.
ABSTRACT NO.: 203: Staged thoracoscopy protocol for achieving primary repair in cases of long gap esophageal atresia: Category: GIT
KrishnanNellaiGoelPrabudhAnandSachitKhannaPuneetBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: nellai93@gmail.com
Objective: To present our protocol of management and results with our indigenous, staged thoracoscopy protocol for achieving primary repair in long-gap esophageal atresia.
Materials and Methods: Prospective study over 4 years on 15 patients of long-gap esophageal-atresia (male: female = 6:9; mean birth weight = 2.15 kg). Neonates with long-gap esophageal-atresia and fit for thoracoscopy were included in this study.
The protocol of management will be discussed in detail.
Results: With this protocol, a delayed primary anastomosis over a #6 infant feeding tube was possible in 10/13 (76.9%) neonates after a mean waiting period of 8.9 weeks (8.3–10.5 weeks). Peroral dye study performed ten (n = 9) or nine (n = 1) days after repair excluded any leak in each of these patients with no hold-up of contrast. Three patients presented with anastomotic narrowing at three (n = 2) and five (n = 1) months postrepair and responded to antegrade dilatation.
Four patients expired, three in the immediate post-operative period (cardiac n = 2, fulminant sepsis n = 1) and one after 4 months of surgery (possible aspiration overnight). The remaining 11 patients are doing well in follow-up (18 months–54 months).
Conclusions: The authors continue to evaluate this protocol based on encouraging results to attempt a primary repair in cases of long-gap esophageal atresia using staged thoracoscopy.
ABSTRACT NO.: 85: Retrospective analysis of the outcome of stoma closure in children without preoperative mechanical bowel preparation: Category: GIT
SaxenaRahulBhattSomyaPathakManishGoelAkhilRathodKirtikumar JSinhaArvindNayakShubhlaxmi RJadhavAvinashDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Jodhpur, Rajasthan, India
E-mail: drrahulsaxena@gmail.com
Introduction: Stoma closure is one of the most frequently performed surgical procedures by pediatric surgeons worldwide. Preoperative mechanical bowel preparation (MBP) has been the standard of care in colorectal surgeries for decades, but the use of MBP is being questioned recently. In this study, we studied the outcome of children undergoing stoma closures without MBP in our department.
Materials and Methods: This is a retrospective observational study of children <18 years undergoing stoma closure from 2017 to 2021. The medical records of patients were reviewed and the primary endpoints such as surgical site infections (SSI), incisional hernia, anastomotic leak, and mortalities were recorded. The categorical data are expressed in percentages and the continuous data are expressed in medians and interquartile ranges. The postoperative complications were classified according to the Clavien−Dindo system.
Results: A total of 89 patients underwent stoma closure without bowel preparation during the study period. The anastomosis leak and incisional hernia were seen in one patient each. The surgical site infections occurred in 23 patients (25.8%), which were superficial in 21 and deep in 2 patients. The Clavien−Dindo Grade 3 complications occurred in 2 (2.2%) patients. The median duration to start feeds and pass first stools was significantly longer in patients with ileostomy closure (P < 0.05).
Conclusion: The outcome of stoma closures without MBP is comparable to that mentioned in the literature with the use of MBP, and hence, the routine use of MBP in stoma closures can be safely avoided in children.
ABSTRACT NO.: 161: Protocol-based management for pediatric functional constipation: Category: GIT
GautamVasuMashalSahilPatelRishavShahShaluMeenaAtulKundalVijayDebnathPinaki RanjanDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, ABVIMS and RML Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: drpinaki_mch@yahoo.co.in
Aims: Pediatric functional constipation (PFC) is diagnosed in the absence of any organic cause of constipation. PFC is often neglected by treating physician and produces lots of anxiety to the parents and patients. Due its nagging tendency and absence of any emergency situation, it is often neglected by the parents. However, protocol-based management of PFC yields satisfactory results, thereby improving the quality of life as well as alleviates the anxiety of the parents.
Methods: A prospective, observational study was conducted from January 2017 to January 2022. The study was conducted over a period of 5 years with the age group of 6 months to 12 years. Sex distribution is equal in both sexes. We excluded the organic causes of constipation by history and examination, patient with red flag sign of constipation are excluded specifically.
The four steps of our protocol-based treatment of PFC are as follows: (1) Disimpaction, (2) Diet modification, (3) Health education, and (4) maintenance therapy.
Results: PFC is widely prevalent in our country due to indulgence in junk food, ignorance of the condition by parents, and to some extent negligence on the part of treating physicians. This is a potentially curable condition with properly instituted protocol-based management and can be offered even in primary healthcare set up. In the absence of red flag sign of constipation such as delayed passage of meconium, abdominal distention, and failure to thrive, a good history and per rectal examination is enough to establish the diagnosis. In some cases, hypothyroidism needs to be excluded. At least 6 months therapy depending on the severity of the case. In our study, nearly 99% patient cured after 6 months to 1 year therapy.
Conclusion: Protocol-based therapy in PFC is very good option to cure this nagging condition and a pediatric surgeon has immense role to treat this neglected disease.
ABSTRACT NO.: 202: Epidemiology and management of foreign body in pediatric age group at a tertiary care center experience over 2 years: Category: GIT
KinjalkMeghnaRatanSimmi KPandaShasanka ShekharNeogiSujoyKumarChiranjivKumarPrafullJainNitinDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, Delhi, India
E-mail: mkinjalk@gmail.com
Background and Objectives: With a greater busy schedule of the parents and restrictions of children within household, the incidence of foreign-body ingestion was observed to have increased in compared to the past. We aimed to identify the demographic profile of foreign body ingested over past 2 years and their treatment course.
Materials and Methods: In a tertiary care center, records of 101 children over the past 2 years were analyzed in the study (January 2020–June 2022). Data were collected for variable such as age, gender, presentation, location of foreign body, their management, and the course during admission till discharge.
Results: The main presenting feature was an accidental ingestion of foreign body observed by bystander and fellow children. Although pain in the abdomen was the complaint in case of complex foreign body which had a complicated course. The ingestion of foreign body was confirmed radiologically. There were 76 boys and 25 girls in the study. The most frequently ingested foreign body was coin in all age groups; with frequency in 1–3 years being -34.2%, 4–6 years -36.2%, 7–9 years – 36.4%, above 10 years - 60%. The location of the foreign body in order of frequency was ileum (n = 43, 42.65%) > jejunum (n = 14, 13.9%) > stomach (n = 18, 17.8%) and > esophagus (n = 6, 5.9%). All the foreign bodies which had crossed double-J (n = 76, 75.25%) were managed conservatively with observation of variable period of minimum 24 h and maximum of 48 h. However, for three cases of such complicated symptomatic foreign body, one with double magnet and two with pins, laparotomy had to be done. The age group of the child ranged between 3 and 6 years for such foreign bodies. In these cases, ileostomy was performed. A total of 21 foreign bodies were retrieved endoscopically, 6 (4: button battery and 2: coin) from esophagus and 15 (3: button battery, 12: coin) from the stomach. 2 button batteries could be managed conservatively.
Conclusion: Overall, the most common gastrointestinal foreign body was coin, even in different age groups. Button battery was the most worrisome foreign body. However, depending on its position, it can be managed conservatively.
ABSTRACT NO.: 270: Gastric pull up in corrosive esophageal injuries: Category: GIT
SinghGarvitaVarmaMuniVarmaVarshaAggarwalSatish KumarDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: garvitasingh429@gmail.com
Introduction: We present our experience with this procedure and challenges experienced in patients with corrosive esophageal injuries, as the extent and severity of involvement may vary from patient to patient.
Materials and Methods: Records of 19 patients (12 girls and 7 boys) who underwent esophageal replacement, between February 2016 and June 2022 were reviewed. One patient underwent colonic replacement while in rest 18, gastric pull up was performed. Five out of 19 patients had a diagnosis of corrosive esophageal injury (rest had tracheoesophageal fistula/esophageal atresia), one due to button battery ingestion, and 4 due to caustic soda ingestion. These corrosive strictures are discussed here.
Cases Details: The age range was 11 months−4 years. The youngest patient, 11 months old female baby had a corrosive button battery ingestion-induced esophageal fistula and esophageal stricture and needed tracheostomy following its endoscopic removal and a feeding jejunostomy with decompressive gastrostomy was performed later. Among the rest four patients having a history of caustic soda ingestion, only one required tracheostomy and all underwent feeding gastrostomy. Patients with multiple failed dilatations posed special problems of perioesphagitis and thick-walled esophagus.
All underwent esophageal replacement with gastric pull up. Retrosternal route was preferred in 2 while in 3 patients, posterior mediastinal route was used. In two patients, a pharyngogastric anastomosis needed to be done in view of long esophageal stricture.
One patient also required excision of clavicular head to facilitate pharyngogastric anastomosis. In the rest three patients, gastroesophageal anastomosis was performed as cervical esophagus was patent and mid/lower esophagus was involved. The native esophagus was resected in all - in three by transhiatal route and in the rest by thoracotomy.
Conclusion: In patients with corrosive esophageal injuries, a multidisciplinary approach should be used and the surgical plan should be individualized taking help of initial bronchoscopy and esophagoscopy findings along with radiological imagings for better outcomes. Esophageal resection is sometimes possible with transmittal route avoiding thoracotomy.
Session - 2: Swapna Dutta Award Papers: ABSTRACT NO.: 11: De novo vesicoureteral reflux following ureterocele decompression in children: A systematic review and meta-analysis comparing laser puncture versus electrosurgical incision techniques: Category: Swapna Dutta Award Paper
SinghApoorvAnandSachitKrishnanNellaiGoelPrabudhYadavDevendra KumarBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Ludhiana, Punjab, India
E-mail: dr.singhapoorv@gmail.com
Aims: The available endoscopic techniques for ureterocele decompression including laser puncture (LP), electrosurgical incision (ES), and cold knife incision, all have comparable success rates. The incidence of de novo vesico ureteric reflux (VUR) varies among these techniques and there is a lack of consensus with limited patients. This systematic review and meta-analysis were performed to define current evidence on comparative efficacy with the special emphasis on de novo VUR.
Methods: The authors searched EMBASE, PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science on November 7, 2021 with keywords (Diathermy OR Laser OR Electrosurgery) AND (Ureterocele). A total of 195 records were identified and duplications were removed. Subsequently, eligibility criteria were applied to relevant 90 studies. Meta-analysis was performed using RevMan 5.4 (Cochrane Collaboration, London) software. As all outcomes were dichotomous, risk ratios (RRs) were calculated and the Mantel-Haenszel method was utilized for pooled RR. The methodological quality was assessed by the Downs and Black Scale.
Results: Five studies were considered for systematic review, while four of them were included in the meta-analysis. A total of 202 children were included with 67 who had developed de novo reflux. Significantly lower rates were noted in LP group vis-a-vis ES group (RR = 0.17, 95% confidence interval [CI] 0.09–0.32, P < 0.00001). Repeat endoscopic decompression (n = 20) was compared for the two groups with RR of 0.66 (95% CI 0.26–1.68) and no significant difference (P = 0.38). A total of 46 secondary procedures was performed in 170 children, mostly ureteral reimplantations, with a significantly higher need following LP versus ES (RR = 0.26, 95% CI 0.13–0.49, P < 0.0001).
Conclusions: Among the available techniques for endoscopic ureterocele decompression, when compared to ES technique, the LP technique is associated with significantly low incidence of de novo VUR and requirement of secondary surgeries (antireflux surgeries). However, due to moderate risk of bias, further trials need to be conducted for an optimal comparison.
ABSTRACT NO.: 131: Innervation preserving sphincter reconstruction in female exstrophy: Technique and results: Category: Swapna Dutta Award Paper
GuptaArchikaKureelSNSunilKanoujiaSinghGurmeetGuptaSurvesh KDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, King George's Medical University, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: drarchika2006@rediffmail.com
Aims: To report the technique and outcome of innervation preserving sphincter reconstruction in female exstrophy repair.
Methods: From 2016 to 2021, 14 cases of female bladder exstrophy with good bladder plate underwent single stage reconstruction along with innervation preserving sphincteroplasty. Bladder plate was mobilized in extraperitoneal plane with the division of intersymphyseal bands. From each clitoral body, skin was degloved to expose neurovascular bundle and Ischiocavernosus muscle identified with muscle simulator. Two parallel incisions from paramedian raphe at vestibule upward parallel to vaginal orifice were deepened to expose bulb of vagina and surrounding contractile bulbovaginalis muscles. After disconnecting both limbs of bulbovaginalis muscles from the medial surface of clitoral body; laid open striated urethral muscle parallel to urethral plate was exposed. Striated urethral muscle was released carefully preserving its innervation for wrapping around reconstructed urethra. Ureteric reimplantation, tubularization of urethral plate with wrapping of sphincter onto urethra, bladder neck repair, bladder closure, clitoroplasty, monsplasty, and abdominal wall closure with transverse innominate osteotomy completed the repair. Outcome measurement included; operative problems, postoperative complications, and continence with upper tract status.
Results: Operative problems included invasion of bulb and bleeding in two, difficult degloving of clitoral body in seven, and suture traction induced injury to clitoral glans in two. Suprapubic fistula occurred in two. Intermittent voiding with dry interval of two hours at night time and at night for 4–6 h was achieved in 11 patients. The necessity of clean intermittent catheterization for complete evacuation was needed in three patients. The upper tract was preserved in all patients.
Conclusions: Careful identification of external urethral sphincter with its preserved innervation and is wrapping around urethra can result in dynamic urethral resistance for intermittent voiding and upper tract preservation.
ABSTRACT NO.: 83: Modified technique of double-J stenting for transperitoneal laparoscopic dismembered pyeloplasty in children: A retrospective cohort study: Category: Swapna Dutta Award Paper
SharmaShyamendra PratapKapoorRohitVermaAnjuNaikPratibha BPujanaKMandeliaAnkurDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, SGPGIMS, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: shyambrd07@gmail.com
Aims: Placing double-J (DJ) stents during transperitoneal laproscopic pyeloplasty in children is often challenging, especially in the early part of the learning curve. The aim of this study was to compare a conventional time tested technique (using guidewire) with a modified technique (without guidewire) of antegrade DJ stent insertion during laproscopic pyeloplasty in children.
Methods: This was a retrospective electronic chart review of all children who underwent transperitoneal laparoscopic pyeloplasty for pelvi-ureteric junction obstruction (PUJO) between 2017 and 2022 in a single surgical unit at our institute. In the first half of the study duration (2017–2019), DJ stent was inserted after the completion of the posterior layer of the pelvi-ureteric anastomosis by the conventional antegrade (CAG) insertion using guidewire (Group A, n = 38). Between 2019 and 2022, antegrade insertion of DJ was done using a modified antegrade technique (MAG) without guidewire (Group B, n = 22).
Results: Fifty-five children (mean age: 8 [2–144] months, mean weight: 5 [3–38] kg, 39 boys, 5 bilateral) with PUJO underwent laparoscopic dismembered pyeloplasty (n = 60) during the study duration. Both the groups were comparable in terms of age, weight, laterality, and preoperative renal parameters. The mean stent insertion time (12 min 50s vs. 10 min 40s), mean operating time (186 min vs. 142 min), and mean number of attempts for stent insertion (1.5 vs. 1.2) were not significantly different for CAG and MAG groups (P > 0.05). No stent malpositioning occurred in the MAG group; in the CAG group, two children had a malpositioned stent in the distal ureter and one child had spontaneous expulsion of stent on postoperative day 2. In 1/22 (4.5%) patients in MAG group, stent could not be inserted without guidewire, and hence, it was inserted by the conventional technique. In 2/38 (5.2%) patients in the CAG group, stent could not be negotiated across the VUJ and alternate modalities were used (1: stentless pyeloplasty, 1: nephrostent insertion). Stent dwell time was 6.5 and 4.5 weeks and mean follow -up duration was 36 and 18 months in CAG and MAG groups, respectively.
Conclusion: We conclude that DJ stenting without guidewire has comparable success and complication rates as the conventional technique with guidewire. With improvement in skills in intra-corporeal maneuvering, the modified technique may be faster and require fewer attempts.
ABSTRACT NO.: 68: Impact of caudal block on postoperative complications in children undergoing hypospadias repair: A retrospective cohort study: Category: Swapna Dutta Award Paper
HegdePriyankaDasSampritiKumarArunJehangirSusanKishoreRaviMathaiJohnDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical Hospital, Vellore, Tamil Nadu, India
E-mail: priyankahegde811@gmail.com
Background and Aims: Urethroplasty surgeries are usually done under general anesthesia with caudal, epidural, and penile blocks used by anesthetists for postoperative analgesia. Many studies claim to have found an association between the increased incidence of complications and perioperative use of caudal blocks. It has been postulated that increased risk of complications is due to the vasodilatation and penile engorgement that occurs after the caudal block. There are only a few studies with limited number of patients focusing on the Indian population. We look back at our 10-year cohort of patients to find a plausible correlation between caudal anesthesia and incidence of complications.
Materials and Methods: Retrospective review of all children who underwent primary urethroplasty from 2011 to 2020 in the Department of Pediatric Surgery, CMC Vellore, was included in the study.
Results: A total of 599 children with the mean age of 4 years were included in the study. Caudal block was given for 418 children and single-shot epidural for 52 children. One hundred and twenty-six children did not receive any regional anesthesia. The incidence of glanular dehiscence was 4% in caudal group as compared 3% in the noncaudal group (P - 0.6) while the incidence of urethrocutaneous fistula was 21% in caudal group compared to 13% in the noncaudal group (P - 0.04). Multivariate regression analysis of level of hypospadias, severity of chordee, surgeon's experience, and type of anesthetic drug used showed no other significant independent variable affecting the outcome.
Conclusion: Postoperative urethral fistula formation was more likely in children who received caudal analgesia but prospective studies are required to validate the outcome.
ABSTRACT NO.: 34: Association between ureteric volume and estimated glomerular filtration function of renal units in new-born babies with hydroureter secondary to posterior urethral valve – “A pilot observational study”: Category: Swapna Dutta Award Paper
BineshAMohananArunDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Government Medical College, Kozhikode, Kerala, India
E-mail: dr.binesh.arayullathil@gmail.com
Aim: Assessing ureteric volume is a novel concept and can accommodate both ureteric diameter and tortuosity. Our study intends to assess the ureteric volume as per non-contrast MRU and evaluate its correlation with renal function in terms of estimated glomerular filtration rate (eGFR).
Methods: A prospective observational study was conducted among new born babies having posterior urethral valve with ureteric diameter >7 mm in USG. Length, S.Cr. at 72 h and ureteric volume as in noncontrast MRU were recorded. eGFR calculated using Brion formula and split to both kidneys based on differential function obtained in DTPA renogram. Analysis was done using STATA software version 12. Pearson correlation coefficient was calculated to examine the association between the ureteric volume and eGFR of corresponding renal unit.
Results and Conclusion: Twenty-six renal units were analyzed. Mean ureteric volume was 4.2 ± 2.6 ml. 30.8% of ureters were refluxing. Positive correlation of moderate strength was found between ureteric diameter and ureteric volume with no statistical significance. Positive correlation was found between the ureteric volume and eGFR of refluxing ureters which was statistically insignificant, probably due to small sample size. This can be explained by the hypothesis that in the refluxing ureters without congenital dysplasia, the brunt of attack due to the increased bladder pressures and vesicoureteral reflux might be taken by the ureters rather than getting transmitted to the renal parenchyma causing increased ureteric volume and better eGFR. This need further confirmation. Benefit of magnetic resonance urography to assess ureteric volume has to be explored further.
ABSTRACT NO.: 271: Correlation of height-to-width ratio of bladder in cystogram with bladder pressure in children with neural tube defect: Category: Swapna Dutta Award Paper
AithalShrilakshmiSaxenaRahulSinhaArvindDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Jodhpur, Rajasthan, India
E-mail: shriaithal96@gmail.com
Introduction: The neurologic effect of neural tube defect on lower urinary tract varies. When the bladder pressure is more than 35–40 cm of H20, ureteral peristalsis does not effectively empty the upper tracts, leading to hydronephrosis and renal insufficiency. Hence, early video urodynamic evaluation and follow-up are essential. Video urodynamic studies are done in few institutions; however, voiding cystourethrogram can be performed in resource-poor settings and interpreted with ease. Although neurogenic bladder appears elongated vertically and with trabeculation, very few studies have shown definite relationship between the shape of the bladder and urodynamic parameters in children with neural tube defect.
Aim: To find out the correlation of the height-to-width ratio (HWR) of the bladder in cystogram with bladder pressure in neural tube defect.
Methods: It is a prospective observational study, being conducted in AIIMS Jodhpur. All the children of neural tube defect, of <18 years of age are included in the study and children with high grade VUR and other bladder pathology are excluded from the study.
Results: The HWR was found to be higher in children with high pressure bladder than in patients low pressure bladder, with mean ratio of 1.32 and 1.15 respectively. This correlates with the clinical judgement of a tall bladder in a neurogenic bladder.
Discussion: Bladder deformity is objectively measured using HWR in cystogram and can be used as a simple screening tool for high pressure bladder.
Free Paper Session - 2: Oncology: ABSTRACT NO.: 338: Prenatal - neonatal tumors: Are they different from the rest?: Category: Oncology
DasKanishkaPatiAkash BihariShubhaAMMohantySuraviMohapatraSonaliSahooBiswajitNaikSupravaSableMukundDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, AIIMS, Bhubaneswar, Odisha, India
E-mail: kanishkadas@hotmail.com
Aim: This is a study of prenatal–neonatal tumors (excluding sacrococcygeal teratomas) and aimed to identify their distinctive features and the impact on their management.
Methods: A retrospective review of the records of the antenatal detection and course, delivery, clinical presentation, management, outcome, and follow-up of all prenatal−neonatal tumors managed in two tertiary teaching centers between 1998 and 2022 was done.
Results: There were 20 patients, 11 males and 9 females. All were term born; only six had an antenatal ultrasonographic detection, most at term. Three were delivered by cesarean section, the rest normally. Majority were symptomatic at birth but sought attention between 1 and 18 months of age. The clinical diagnosis differed from the final diagnosis in over half. Most were benign, some of questionable malignant potential (kaposiform hemangioendothelioma and hemangiopericytoma) and some frankly malignant (congenital fibrosarcoma and neuroblastoma). Tumor markers, imaging, and trucut biopsy-guided individualized management that ranged from observation for regression (neuroblastoma), medication (steroids for hemangioendothelioma), or surgical intervention with/without adjuvant chemoradiotherapy in the rest. Two did not submit to further management and three have succumbed to progressive disease. At follow-up (1–18 years), 15 are well and disease free.
Conclusion: The embryogenesis/tumor biology of prenatal neonatal tumors dictated their treatment and majority had a favorable outcome with judicious management. Controversies pertaining to the practical management of these special tumors (counseling, histopathology, chemoradiotherapy concerns, reconstruction, and rehabilitation) are discussed.
ABSTRACT NO.: 80: Outcomes of laparoscopically managed Wilm's tumor: Category: Oncology
SehgalMehakBajpaiMinuAnandSachitDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: mehak.sehgal91@gmail.com
Background: In the present study, we aim to review the outcomes of children with Wilm's tumor (WT) operated through the minimally invasive surgery approach at our center. We also intend to highlight the essential surgical steps during laparoscopic excision of large WTs.
Methods: This retrospective study included children with unilateral WT who had undergone resection over a period of 4 years, w.e.f., July 2013 to July 2017. Simple maneuvers such as tilting the table in different positions use of blunt metallic cannula to lift the tumor to access the hilar vessels, etc., were used to dissect large WT. An extended-lumbotomy incision was used for tumor retrieval and lymph node sampling.
Results: Eleven patients (M:F = 7:4) of WT, all having Stage III disease, had undergone laparoscopic tumor resection at our center during the study period. The median age at presentation was 36 months (range = 17 months-5 years) and the median preoperative tumor volume was 1140 (range = 936–1560) cm2. The average length of the lumbotomy incision was 6.3 (range = 5–8.2) cm. The median hospital stay was 6 (range = 5–10) days. Two children developed complications (port-site recurrence and Grade III surgical site infection in one each) during the postoperative period. All cases are long-term survivors with a median follow-up of 86 (range = 56–104) months.
Conclusion: The present study highlights the feasibility and safety of the removal of large WT through the MIS approach. Problems due to large-sized tumors in children can be overcome by simple maneuvers. Furthermore, adequate lymph node sampling is possible with a suitably placed extended lumbotomy incision for tumor removal.
ABSTRACT NO.: 164: Surgical approach to pediatric tumor of mediastinum and cervico-thoracic junction with critical vascular involvement: Category: Oncology
ChackoBenjaminNabeelQZameerMMVinayCRaoSanjayD’CruzAshley JDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: benjaminchacko@gmail.com
Aim: To describe the different surgical approaches to pediatric cervico-thoracic and mediastinal tumor: clampshell or trapdoor thoracotomy.
To evaluate the outcomes of trapdoor or clampshell thoracotomy: adequacy of surgical resection and complication.
Methods: All pediatric patients with mediastinal tumors undergoing clampshell or trapdoor thoracotomy for tumor excision between March 2020 and March, 2022 were included in the study. All were evaluated with preoperative imaging. Uniform surgical approach was in all cases. For a trap-door thoracotomy, transverse incision made superior to the clavicle, descending to the mid-portion of suprasternal notch continuing downward midline sternum to fifth intercostals space up-to anterior axillary line; while clampshell thoracotomy was done by making a curvilinear incision along the fourth intercostals space bilaterally from each anterior axillary line, connecting at the midline. Patients were followed up to 30 days postsurgery, documenting adequacy of resection and all complications.
Results: Our cohort consisted of five patients (aged between 14 months and 11 years) of whom, three had neuroblastoma and two had germ cell tumor. One patient had tumor involving bilateral hemithorax and four cervico-thoracic tumors. All tumors were in-close proximity and encasing major vessels of the neck. Four underwent trap-door thoracotomy while one had clampshell thoracotomy. All had near-total gross resection of tumors with no intra-operative complications. Four were extubated within 24 h and one on POD2. Two patients developed diaphragm palsy but none requiring surgery. Two had lung collapse postoperatively, one requiring bronchoscopy and suction of mucus plug. No patient required re-exploration. Average LOH was 7.2 days (with the earliest discharge being 4 days.) Two patients developed Horner's syndrome.
Conclusion: Trap-door and clampshell thoracotomy approach offer better exposure for near-total resection. Most postoperative complications could be managed conservatively and re-intervention in our cohort was nil. However, it still needs long-term follow-up to assess the development of thoracic and restrictive lung pathologies.
ABSTRACT NO.: 81: Long-term ototoxic effects of cisplatin in the survivors of pediatric solid tumors: Category: Oncology
SehgalMehakJainVisheshAgarwalaSandeepDhuaAnjanThakarAlokBakhshiSameerKalaivaniManiDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: mehak.sehgal91@gmail.com
Aim: Cisplatin is an integral component of chemotherapeutic regimes of childhood tumors, but their use may impart a significant hearing deficit in long-term survivors. We aim to evaluate the long-term auditory effects of cisplatin in survivors of pediatric solid tumors.
Materials and Methods: The study included a cohort of survivors of hepatoblastoma (HB) and malignant germ cell tumors (MGCT) registered at pediatric surgical-oncology clinic from 1994 to 2016. The ototoxic effects of cisplatin were evaluated by pure-tone audiometry (PTA) done during follow-up.
Results: A total 129 patients were deemed as survivors, with a mean duration of follow-up in HB and MGCT of 8.47 (SD 4.27) years and 7.84 (SD 4.25) years. Of the 117 survivors receiving cisplatin, one patient had hearing difficulty at follow-up. PTA was performed in 83 survivors, of which an audiogram was available for 51 survivors for evaluation, 28 (54.9%) of which were reported normal. Hearing loss was detected in 39 (76.4%) out of 51 survivors by the SIOP grading system. Severe hearing loss was detected in 32 (62.7%) survivors (grade 3, 4). Hearing loss was detected in 26 (51%) survivors according to the Brock's grading system. Severe hearing loss was detected in 6 (11.7%) survivors. The survivors of MGCT had a significantly higher (92.3%) chance at developing hearing loss compared to 60% of HB survivors (P = 0.012). A dose of cisplatin of ≥420 mg/m2 was found to be associated with hearing loss, with a sensitivity of 75% and specificity of 66.67%. The mean age at diagnosis was significantly higher in children with hearing loss (48.50, SD 47.54 months) as compared to children without hearing loss (24.5, SD 32.12 months) (P = 0.01928). Age at audiometry did not correlate with hearing loss.
Conclusions: The SIOP grading system, being more sensitive, must be uniformly used to screen for hearing loss. Older age at diagnosis, higher dose, and MGCT survivors need close monitoring.
ABSTRACT NO.: 188: Three-dimensional printing in children with extra-cranial solid tumors: A systematic review: Category: Oncology
KrishnanNellaiAnandSachitGoelPrabudhDhuaAnjan KumarJainVisheshYadavDevendra KumarBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: nellai93@gmail.com
Aim: This systematic review aimed to summarize the current evidence on the utility of three-dimensional (3D) printing in pediatric extra-cranial solid tumors.
Methods: PubMed, Embase, SCOPUS, and Google Scholar databases were explored with appropriate search criteria to select the relevant studies. Data were extracted to study the bibliographic information of each article, the number of patients in each study, age of the patient(s), type of tumor, organ of involvement, and application of 3D printing (surgical planning, training, and/or parental education). The details of 3D printing, such as type of imaging used, software details, printing technique, printing material, and cost were also extracted.
Results: Eight studies were finally included in the systematic review. 3D printing technology was used in 30 children; which included Wilm's tumor (n = 13), neuroblastoma (n = 7), hepatic tumors (n = 8), retroperitoneal tumor (n = 1), and synovial sarcoma (n = 1). Among the included studies, the technology was utilized for preoperative surgical planning (five studies), improved understanding of the surgical anatomy of solid organs (two studies), and improving the parental understanding of the tumor and its management (one study). Computed tomography and magnetic resonance imaging were either performed alone or in combination for radiological evaluation in these children. Different types of printers and printing materials were used in the included studies. The cost of the 3D printed models and time involved (range 10 h to 4–5 days) were reported by two studies each.
Conclusions: 3D printed models can be of great assistance to pediatric surgeons in understanding the spatial relationships of tumors with the adjacent anatomic structures. They also facilitate the understanding of families, improving doctor–patient communication.

Conclusion: Extragonadal teratomas are relatively rare in children. They can have varied presentation. Complete excision usually has good result.
ABSTRACT NO.: 82: Anthropometric outcomes in survivors of pediatric solid tumors: Category: Oncology
SehgalMehakJainVisheshAgarwalaSandeepDhuaAnjanBakhshiSameerKalaivaniManiDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: mehak.sehgal91@gmail.com
Introduction: The importance of nutritional status in childhood cancer patients cannot be overemphasized, not only due to its potential impact on the disease and survival, but also for its impact on their survivorship. We sought to evaluate the long-term arthropometric outcomes in pediatric solid tumor survivors.
Materials and Methods: The study included a cohort of pediatric solid tumor survivors registered at pediatric surgical oncology clinic from 1994 to 2016. Anthropometry was noted at the time of presentation, after the completion of chemotherapy, and at last follow-up. The z-scores for weight-for-age and height-for-age were calculated using the WHO growth charts for age <5 years and Indian Academy of Pediatrics growth charts for age ≥5 years.
Results: Of the survivors, 317 survivors, comprising of 48, 81, and 188 survivors of hepatoblastoma (HB), malignant germ cell tumors (MGCT), and WT, respectively, were included in the analysis. The median age at diagnosis was 24.5 (interquartile range 59–13.2) months, with range of 5–19.54 years of follow-up. On evaluating the z-scores of the collective cohort of survivors at three time points, we found that the z-scores for height for age, weight for age, and body mass index (BMI) showed an improving trend in nutrition. The difference in the prevalence of malnutrition and severe malnutrition was found to be statistically significant when any two time points were compared during follow-up. A similar trend was seen in the individual cohorts of HB, MGCT, and WT, which showed 10% and 7.5%, 10.9% and 9%, 15.7%, and 11.4% to be stunted and wasted at last follow-up. We found no significant effect of age at diagnosis on the anthropometric measures at last follow-up. On evaluating BMI in 28 adult survivors, we found 43% to be in the underweight category.
Conclusions: Anthrometric measures improve during follow-up, however, up to 15% children persist being malnourished which is close to the national averages. Thus, the effect of therapy improves over time.
Session - 8: U. C. Chakraborthy Award Papers: ABSTRACT NO.: 35: A decade of research in pediatric surgery: What garners the most citations?: Category: U. C. Chakraborthy Award Paper
BanerjeeArkaBendrePradnya SDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, B J Wadia Hospital for Children, Mumbai, Maharashtra, India
E-mail: arkabanerjee6989@gmail.com
Introduction: The citation count of a particular article indicates its influence within the readership. A survey of the top 10 articles (by citations/year) in pediatric surgery from each of the three leading international journals (Journal of Pediatric Surgery [JPS], European Journal of Pediatric Surgery [EurJPS], and Pediatric Surgery International [PSI]) and the foremost Indian journal (Journal of Indian Association of Pediatric Surgeons [JIAPS]) should give an insight into the research that has been quoted the most in the last decade.
Through an extensive literature search, this paper aims to identify, analyze, and categorize the characteristics of the most cited papers in pediatric surgery in the last decade, worldwide and in India.
Methods: The number of citations of all articles from JPS, EurJPS, PSI, and JIAPS published between January 2013 and May 2022 were sorted using the “Publish or Perish®” software (version 8). The top 10 articles from each journal (by citations/year) were then tabulated and details including the topic studied, citations/year, year of publication, and type of study were noted.
Results: There were 13 review articles and 27 original articles – 12 retrospective reviews, 6 consensus papers, 6 surveys, and 3 animal research studies. 15 of these articles were from the United States of America, 12 were from Europe and 3 were global, spanning continents. Among the top 10 cited papers from JIAPS, 8 were from India. The most commonly covered topics were esophageal atresia, necrotizing enterocolitis, acute appendicitis, and Hirschsprung's disease.
Conclusion: The most influential manuscripts relate to common neonatal surgical conditions and appendicitis. This information should be of value to upcoming pediatric surgeons to choose their prospective research and create a long-lasting impact on the field of pediatric surgery.

Aim: The aim of this study is to evaluate the role of the urinary biomarkers in the diagnosis and management as the early markers of progressive kidney damage in children with congenital uropathies of kidney and urinary tract (CAKUT) viz a viz the conventional markers of renal injury. ABSTRACT NO.: 251: Effect of cytomegalovirus infection on initial presentation and overall prognosis of biliary atresia patients: Category: U. C. Chakraborthy Award Paper

Background and Aim: Biliary atresia is known to have a multifactorial etiology and perinatal infection with hepatotropic viruses such as cytomegalovirus is a probable trigger in a subset of patients. The aim of the current study is to evaluate the effects of CMV association of biliary atresia on the initial presentation of patients and their response to Kasai portoenterostomy.
Methods: We conducted a retrospective, single center study on 20 patients of biliary atresia and classified them into two groups based on their CMV IgM positivity. We compared the age of initial presentation, the liver biochemistry at presentation, immediate and delayed follow-up, rate of jaundice clearance following Kasai portoenterostomy and histopathology of the liver between the two groups. Data were reported in terms of means and a P < 0.05 was considered statistically significant.
Results: Out of 20 cases of biliary atresia, 60% (n = 12) were CMV IgM positive. The rate of resolution of jaundice post Kasai portoenterostomy was also evidently less in CMV-positive patients. Four out of 12 patients has a bilirubin >2 mg/dl at 6 months’ follow-up.
Conclusion: CMV-associated biliary atresia patients have delayed initial presentation and impaired jaundice clearance post-Kasai portoenterostomy. Role of antiviral therapy should be studied in this subset of patients.
ABSTRACT NO.: 274: To study the diagnostic accuracy of voiding cystourethrography for the detection of residual valves after endoscopic ablation of posterior urethral valves: Category: U. C. Chakraborthy Award Paper
SinghGarvitaAggarwalSatish KumarPrasadAlpanaSaxenaKKDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Sir Ganga Ram Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: garvitasingh429@gmail.com
Methods: Sixty patients of posterior urethral valve (mean age: 17.59 months with range: 4 days−12 years) who underwent primary cystoscopic valve ablation between Aug’ 2018 and June 2022 were studied. They were evaluated by history, examination, and a voiding cystourethrogram (VCUG)/on table expression cystourethrogram after 3–4 months of valve ablation. On VCUG, posterior urethra: anterior urethra ratio (PAR) was calculated. PAR >2.2 was taken as indicative of residual valves. All cases then underwent check cystoscopy by the senior author who was blinded to the VCUG findings. Using hook electrode on resectoscope, any hookable membrane at the site of previous ablation was considered as residual valves and ablated. If no hookable membrane was found, the valves were considered as completely ablated. Patients with residual valves were further followed up for a check cystoscopy and VCUG after 3–6 months.
Results: At diagnosis, the PAR varied between 0.89 and 15.59 with mean PAR being 3.54 (±2.3). At first follow-up 3–4 months after valve ablation, 26 out of 60 patients had a PAR >2.2, indicating presence of residual valves, while the rest 34 had PAR <2.2. Check cystoscopy was then performed and residual valves were found in 47 patients. In 25 out of 47, the PAR was >2.2. In rest 22, the PAR was <2.2. Diagnostic accuracy of VCUG for residual valves came out to be 61.67% with sensitivity of 53.19% and specificity of 92.31%. VCUG had a high positive predictive value of 96.15% and a negative predictive value of 35.29% for diagnosing residual valves. In patients who had no residual valve, the mean PAR was 1.61, ranging from 0.86 to 2.4. While in patients with residual valves, the mean PAR came out to be 2.63, ranging from 0.8 to 7.84.
Conclusions: In our study, 78.3% cases had residual valves on check cystoscopy, while only 43.3% of them were correctly predicted by VCUG. Hence, VCUG alone is a weak predictor of residual valves. To detect residual valves, check cystoscopy is recommended in all patients following primary valve ablation.
ABSTRACT NO.: 373: Vital dye lymphoscintigraphy: An answer to intraoperative decision-making between staged or single-staged laparoscopic S-F intraoperatively: Category: U. C. Chakraborthy Award Paper
NandanRuchiraKumarDeepakPandeyVaibhavDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Banaras Hindu University, Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: ruchira.nandan@gmail.com
Introduction: We hypothesize that vital dye lymphoscintigraphy can be used to assess the testicular supply through vessel to vas and can be utilized to decide whether go for stage of two staged S-F operation. A prospective comparative study was performed to compare the clinical outcomes of children treated with one-stage Fowler-Stephens (FSI) versus two stage FSI procedures and to assess the role of vital dye lymphoscintigraphy technique.
Methods: A prospective comparative study was done to include all patients with intraabdominal testis above 6 months of age. All patients were subjected to physical examination, scrotum and groin ultrasound and color Doppler of testis, hormonal study (AMH, Estradiol, FSH, Testosterone), and other associated necessary examinations. The patients were subjected to the study protocol during the laparoscopic S-F operation. On laparoscopy, methylene blue (0.5–1 ml) lymphoscintigraphy was performed. Methylene blue was injected by a 27F spinal needle into testicular capsule and flow of methylene blue was observed for 10 min. Depending upon the flow of methylene blue, patients were categorized as definitive, intermediate, or no flow along the vas. Patients were randomized for single stage or two stage F-S operation by the chit system. All the patients who fulfilled the protocol were included in the end for the interpretation of the outcome. Patients who were lost to follow-up at least 6 months were excluded from the study. In cases of unilateral UDT with postoperative scrotal ultrasound, a postoperative relative testis atrophy percentage was calculated using the volumetric measurements provided by the ultrasound.
Observations: Lymphatic mapping was done in 39 testes, of which in FS1 group, 2 testes showed no flow, 3 showed intermediate, and 10 showed definitive flow toward vas. In two stage group, 4 testes showed no flow, 5 showed intermediate, and 8 showed definitive flow toward vas.
There was significant increase in testicular volume in both groups which was comparable. The follow-up TAI at 6 months and 12 months decreased significantly in both the groups and was comparable. The mean testosterone level showed a significant increase at both the follow-ups and was comparable in both the groups.
Conclusion: The outcome of single-stage and two-stage S-F operation was similar in terms of testicular volume, testicular atrophy index, and hormonal response. Methylene blue lymphoscintigraphy can be used to assess the blood flow to testis from vessel to vas, thus should be used intraoperatively for deciding between the single and two stage SF procedures.
Session - 9: K. K. Sharma Award Papers: ABSTRACT NO.: 57: Isoperistaltic gastric tube: Our experience and follow-up outcomes: Category: K. K. Sharma Award Paper
KrishnanNellaiBajpaiMinuGoelPrabudhAnandSachitDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: nellai93@gmail.com
Aim: To present our experience and follow-up outcomes with isoperistaltic gastric tube (IGT) for esophageal substitution.
Methods: All patients who underwent esophageal replacement with IGT from 2007 to 2019 were prospectively followed up. Clinical symptoms, growth parameters, and complications were noted. Cine esophagogram, 24 h pH monitoring, and gastric tube manometry were carried out to assess the physiology and function of the neotube.
Results: Sixteen patients (12 males) underwent esophageal replacement for various indications (corrosive stricture = 10 and esophageal atresia = 6). The average age at surgery was 58 months (range 10 months – 17 years). One patient had cyanotic heart disease and died 1 month following the surgery due to congestive cardiac failure. Esophagogastric anastomosis leak occurred in six cases. Only one of these required diversion. There was no graft necrosis. One child had mild dysphagia and responded well to balloon dilatation. After an average follow-up of 40 months (range 6 month-80 months), all patients were accepting feeds showed significant weight gain without any reflux-related symptoms. Acidic pH was observed in the gastric tube (mean 4.5). The cine esophagogram revealed smooth contrast passage into the neo-tube with a long abdominal component, prograde peristaltic activity and normal gastric emptying (average neo-esophageal emptying time 6.7 ± 3.2 s). Manometry revealed variable peristaltic activity within the gastric tube (distal neo-esophageal amplitude = 69.3 ± 4.2 mm Hg and proximal neo-esophageal amplitude = 26.7 ± 2.1 mm Hg).
Conclusions: The IGT is a safe and reliable option for esophageal replacement with minimal complications. The neo tube shows evidence of peristaltic activity suggesting that it acts more than a mere conduit for food and may be responsible for better feed tolerance and lesser complications. The longer length of the tube achieved by this technique acts as a potent anti-reflux mechanism.
ABSTRACT NO.: 74: Correlation of hepatic shear-wave elastography with liver histology and postoperative biliary drainage in patients of biliary atresia: Category: K. K. Sharma Award Paper
AhmadMd. FahimKanojiaRavi PSolankiShaileshSamujhRamSaxenaAkshay KGuptaKirtiLalSadhna BDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, PGIMER, Chandigarh, Punjab, India

Aim: To assess the role of hepatic shear-wave elastography (SWE) as a noninvasive method in identifying liver fibrosis and cirrhosis in patients of Extrahepatic Biliary Atresia.
