ABSTRACT NO.: 113: Association of magnetic resonance imaging findings with continence score after surgery for anorectal malformation: Category: K. K. Sharma Award Paper
ChakrabortySaibalRatanSimmi KPandaShasanka ShekarNeogiSujoyDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Maulana Azad Medical College, New Delhi, India
E-mail: saibalch2002.dsc@gmail.com
Aims: To associate the findings of magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) pelvis with preoperative rectal pouch position by Wingspread classification and postoperative Kelly's continence score in children who had completed all stages of anorectal malformation (ARM) repair.
Methods: In this observational study conducted at Lok Nayak Hospital, 30 consecutive children, aged 3−12 years, were evaluated at least 6 months after the completion of all stages of surgery for the correction of ARM. Pouch colon, spinal problems, common cloaca, and re-do cases were excluded from the study. All children underwent MRI pelvis to assess for the parameters known to affect postoperative fecal continence - bowel diameter, placement of rectum in the smooth muscle complex (central/eccentric), the degree of development of striated muscle complex (SMC) complex (good/intermediate/poor), and anorectal angle. Continence was assessed clinically by Kelly's scoring. Correlation of MRI finding with three groups of Kelly's score was computed.
Results: Male-to-female ratio was 5:1 with high (n = 23) and intermediate type (n = 7), respectively, by Wingspread classification. Among MRI findings, pulled through rectum was found to be in center of the muscle complex except one. No statistical significance (P = 0.355) was found between pulled through bowel diameter (3.02 ± 0.96 cm; 3.2 ± 0.87 cm, and 3.4 ± 0.70 cm in good, moderate, and poor Kelly's group, respectively) with continence classes. The development of SMC had significant association with Kelly's score (P < 0.0001) as well as preoperative rectum position. Significant association (P = 0.001) was found between anorectal angle with Kelly's score. On multivariate analysis only, anorectal angle was found to be have significant association with continence score among all the MRI pelvis parameters with mean angle less than 115 degree having best continence status.
Conclusion: Fecal continence assessed by Kelly's score had significant positive association with muscle complex development and anorectal angle. However, among all factors, anorectal angle emerged out to be the strongest predictor.
ABSTRACT NO.: 171: Do children catch-up growth after liver transplantation?: Category: K. K. Sharma Award Paper
TiwariAvantikaNabeelAQZameerMMVinayCRaoSanjayD’CruzAshleyDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: avantika28tiwari@gmail.com
Aim: To study growth in children after liver transplantation on long-term follow-up and to analyze the various risk factors that might affect growth posttransplantation.
Materials and Methods: This is a prospective longitudinal cohort study of children who have completed 5 years’ follow-up after liver transplantation at our center. The study period was from January 2006 to May 2022. Data were collected from a continuously updated, prospective database. Height and weight at the time of transplantation were recorded. Median height and weight with interquartile range posttransplantation were plotted against time at 1 year, 3 years, and 5 years follow-up. Risk factors affecting growth posttransplantation were studied - (1) Etiology (biliary atresia or nonbiliary atresia); (2) Type of immunosuppression (double or triple drug regimen); (3) Re-admissions within a year of transplantation; (4) Epstein−Barr viremia; and (5) posttransplant lymphoproliferative disorder (PTLD). Friedman test and two-way ANOVA using post hoc Dunn test were conducted for multiple comparisons and P < 0.05 was considered statistically significant.
Results: A total of 94 patients underwent liver transplantation. Forty-eight patients who completed 5 years’ follow-up were studied. Thirty-five were male and 13 were female. Median age at the time of transplant was 25 months. Median height and weight were 78 cm and 10 kg at the time of transplant, respectively (<3rd centile). Accelerated growth in both height and weight was noted after 1 year of transplantation where the median height and weight were 94 cm and 13 kg, respectively (between 10th and 25th centile). Catch-up growth was noted at the end of 3 years, where median height and weight were 107 cm and 16.5 kg, respectively, (at 50th centile). By the end of 5 years, continuous linear growth in both height and weight was noted (median height and weight was 120 cm and 22 kg, respectively). Children with biliary atresia showed continuous linear growth posttransplantation, but it was less than those without biliary atresia, although the results were statistically insignificant. Other risk factors such as immunosuppression, EB viremia, PTLD, and re-admissions within 1 year posttransplant had no significant effect on the height and weight.
Conclusion: Liver transplant in childhood offers excellent opportunity for catch-up growth. Rapid growth is seen in the first-year posttransplantation. Catch-up growth was noted at the end of 3 years and was sustained until the fifth year after transplant. Etiology, re-admissions, infections, and immunosuppression had no significant effect on the overall growth.
ABSTRACT NO.: 195: Implementing enhanced recovery after surgery protocols in pediatric patients undergoing choledochal cyst excision: Category: K. K. Sharma Award Paper
ViswanathanNiveditha ShamaLoganathanArun KumarHomiSusan JehangirKishoreBSS RaviMathaiJohnDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamil Nadu, India
E-mail: nivi.here@gmail.com
Aims and Objectives: Enhanced recovery after surgery (ERAS) includes protocols enacted preoperatively, intraoperatively, and postoperatively aimed at optimal physiological recovery after surgery. ERAS is well established in adults but has not been well studied in children. We created an ERAS guideline and examined the effects of its pilot implementation in pediatric patients treated for choledochal cyst.
Materials and Methods: We performed a prospective case–control study utilizing an ERAS protocol in patients aged less than15 years undergoing surgery for choledochal cyst. Protocol elements included: multimodal analgesia, opioid minimization, routine nausea prophylaxis, avoidance of catheters and drains, early enteral feeding, and early ambulation. ERAS patients were propensity matched with historical controls. The outcomes of interest included opioid usage, time to full enteral feeds, length of stay (LOS), cost, readmission, and complication rates.
Results: ERAS group included eight patients with a mean age of 5.8 years, whereas control group had ten patients with mean age of 4.6 years. All patients in ERAS group underwent hepatico-duodenostomy with one child operated by laparoscopic approach. Control group had three patients who had hepaticojejunostomy with minimal access used in three patients. The median LOS decreased from 7 days to 4 days in ERAS group (P = 0.01). There was an associated decrease in the median time to first feeds (1 day vs. 3 days), median time to full enteral feeds (2 days vs. 5 days), and mean dose of opioids used. The overall cost was also lower in the ERAS group compared to the historical cohort (Rs. 85,630 vs. Rs. 96,179). There were no reported complications or 30 day readmissions in the ERAS group.
Conclusion: Our results indicate that the implementation of ERAS protocols is safe and feasible in pediatric gastrointestinal surgery. They can improve patient comfort, shorten the duration of stay, reduce costs, and accelerate postoperative rehabilitation without increasing the risk of postoperative complications.
ABSTRACT NO.: 323: Liver histopathology in pediatric patients with choledochal cyst: Category: K. K. Sharma Award Paper
MayankKumarChoudhuryS RoySinghSmitaSarinYKDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Lady Hardinge Medical College, Kalawati Saran Children's Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: roychouin@yahoo.co.in
Aims: To study the various histopathological changes in the liver in pediatric patients with choledochal cyst (CC) and to correlate the changes with the types of the cyst and clinical presentation.
Materials and Methods: In this prospective observational study, all children up to the age of 18 years with CC operated in a tertiary care children's hospital were enrolled. Patients with previous interventions were excluded. Detailed clinical history and the demographic parameters (age and gender), symptoms (pain, lump, jaundice, vomiting, and fever) and signs (lump, hepatomegaly, icterus, fever, features of portal hypertension, and liver failure), and preoperative imaging (ultrasonography, contrast-enhanced computed tomography, and magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography) were recorded. Excised CC along with liver biopsy was sent for histopathological examination. Histopathological changes in the liver included hepatocellular damage, bile duct proliferation, cholestasis, and liver fibrosis. Liver changes were correlated with clinical parameters.
Results: A total number of 24 cases of CC were included: age (mean 2.5 year) and sex (M:F 10:14). There were 20 cystic and 4 fusiform type of CC. Liver histopathological changes of varying severity were evident in all patients. Hepatocellular damage was seen in 14/24 (58%) patients, bile duct proliferation was seen in 4/24 (16%) patients, cholestasis was seen in 3/24 (12%) patients, and biliary fibrosis was seen in 3/24 (12%) patients. No correlation was found between the age and type of the cyst with liver histological changes.
Conclusion: Histopathological changes in the liver in the form of hepatocellular damage, bile duct proliferation, and cholestasis were found in all cases of CC. Liver fibrosis was rare. The significance of those changes will require further studies including larger number of patients and long-term follow-up.
Free Paper Session - 3: Neonatal: ABSTRACT NO.: 22: Assessing the feasibility of predicting intermediate outcomes of esophageal atresia using Okamoto classification: A prospective study: Category: Neonatal
DeySantoshRamanCol. VSDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Army Hospital RR, New Delhi, India
E-mail: santodey@gmail.com
Aims: Assessing the feasibility of predicting intermediate outcomes of esophageal atresia using Okamoto classification.
Materials and Methods: Sixty-nine patients of esophageal atresia (EA+/−) tracheoesophageal fistula (TEF) were followed for 1 year after discharge following surgery. Morbidity and mortality outcomes were compared for the various Okamoto classes.
Results: There were 40, 15, 10, and 4 patients in Okamoto I, II, III, and IV, respectively. There was significant correlation between the Okamoto classes when the incidence of poor weight gain (P = 0.001), lower respiratory tract infection (LRTI) (P = 0.007), and failure to thrive (P = 0.01) was compared between the classes. Stricture rate did not show any significant correlation (P = 0.94). 1-year overall survival was 82%, 53.3%, 60%, and 25% in Okamoto I, II, III, and IV, respectively. Okamoto class I demonstrated a significantly better survival than class II (P = 0.02) and class IV (P = 0.003). However, the difference between class I and III was not significant (P = 0.17).
Conclusion: Although Okamoto risk classification was made to predict the survival of EA+/− TEF patients after primary surgery, this study shows that even during follow-up period of one year, there is a significant correlation between the various Okamoto classes and outcome in terms of poor weight gain, LRTI, failure to thrive, and mortality. Hence, this classification can also help prognosticate the intermediate outcomes.
ABSTRACT NO.: 97: Early versus late transanastomotic feeding in neonates with tracheoesophageal fistula: Category: NEONATAL
TotadriVarsha MKanodiaShrishtiVinodYVSShelkeVinayakumarAcharyaSamir KantPuriArchanaJadhavAmitSugandhiNidhiDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Vardhman Mahavir Medical College and Safdarjung Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: varsha2892@gmail.com
Introduction: Controversy exists over right time to start transanastomotic feeds in operated patients of esophageal atresia and tracheoesophageal fistula (EATEF). Early feeds, though beneficial for nutrition and recovery, are believed to increase the risk of leak and stricture. We aimed to compare the incidence of anastomotic leak and stricture in patients with EATEF, receiving early vs late transanastomotic feeds.
Materials And Methods: Prospective randomized controlled study from 2019 - 2021. All operated patients of EATEF were randomly divided into early feeds (Day 2: Group A) and delayed feeds (Day 5: Group B). Clinical evidence of leak was noted and upper GI dye study was done on POD 7 and at 6 weeks to identify the incidence of leak and stricture. Fischer t-test was applied to statistically analyze the differences between the two groups.
Results: Twenty-nine neonates (11 males and 18 females) undergoing primary repair of EATEF were included. Sixteen (Group A) received early feeds and 13 (Group B) received delayed feeds. On POD 7, upper GI dye study done showed anastomotic leak in 1 of 16 (6.25%) in Group A and in 3 of 13 (23.08%) in Group B (p=0.2990). UGI dye study at 6 weeks showed stricture in 5 of 16 (31%) from Group A and 6 of 13 (46.1%) from Group B (p=0.41).
Conclusion: There is no significant difference in the outcome between early and delayed transanastomotic feeds. Early feeds should be considered as it negates the complications associated with prolonged parenteral nutrition.
Keywords: Anastomotic leak, stricture, transanastomotic feeds
ABSTRACT NO.: 98: Review of a very low birth weight (VLBW) series of esophageal atresia and successful primary repair in a neonate weighing 525 g: Category: Neonatal
JoshiAshwiniKelayArunMorsiAhmedDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Royal London Hospital, Parts Health NHS, London, UK
E-mail: ashwini.joshi2@nhs.net
Aim of the Study: Advances in neonatal intensive care are securing the survival of an ever-growing cohort of very and extremely low birth weight neonates. Inevitably, a proportion will face the further challenge of complex surgical anomalies such as esophageal atresia. We sought to determine the outcome of this high-risk group at our center.
Methods: Retrospective single-center review of patient records for very- or extremely low birth weight neonates with esophageal atresia over a 16-year period from August 2003 to September 2019. Data were collected regarding gestation, birth weight, associated anomalies, operative interventions, postoperative course, and survival. Patients were subdivided according to the modified Spitz prognostic classification. Local ethical study approval was gained.
Results: Over the study period, 17 (13%) of 128 neonates with esophageal atresia had a birth weight <1500 g. Median gestational age was 30 weeks (range 24–34) with a median birth weight of 1255 g (range 525–1435 g). All had Gross type C anatomy. Initial fistula ligation and gastrostomy formation were performed in three with subsequent delayed primary anastomosis.
Survival was 93% for Spitz group II and 100% for the two patients in Spitz group IV. The one mortality was secondary to refractory pulmonary hypertension in a 555 g patient. The smallest survivor - 30/40 gestation, birthweight 525 g – now aged 5, has co-morbidity associated with prematurity including severe learning difficulties, deafness, and food aversion necessitating gastrostomy feeds.
Conclusion: In the modern era, birth weight exerts a far diminished influence on survival in esophageal atresia, with definitive repair possible in the first days of life. This population mandates holistic, multidisciplinary care to optimize the outcomes.
 ABSTRACT NO.: 122: Challenges in the managements of neonatal oral masses: Category: Neonatal
SinghSudhirPantNitinTyagiNirpexRawatJiledarDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, King George's Medical University, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: sudhir@kgmcindia.edu
Aims: To highlight the challenges of managements of neonatal oral masses cases at our center.
Methods: Records of neonates with oral masses from 2016 to 2021 were reviewed with respect to demographic profile, clinicoradiological findings, management, follow-up, and outcome.
Results: Total of seven cases, M:F = 5:2. The mean age of presentation was 6 days (1 day-15 days). Presentations were feeding difficulty (n = 3), respiratory distress (n = 2), and cosmetic disfigurement of face in all cases. Plain X-ray done in all cases, contrast-enhanced computed tomography done in four cases. Origin of masses from upper jaw (n = 4), lateral part of tongue along with lower jaw (n = 1), and from nasopharynx (n = 2). Excision of mass done in six cases. One patient had difficult intubation. One patient expired preoperatively due to sepsis. Histopathology mature teratoma in two cases and melanotic neuroectodermal tumor in one case. All three operated cases were doing well in follow-up period, no recurrence.
Conclusion: Neonatal oral masses are rare and the presentation can have varied. Securing the airways is the important critical step. Surgical resection is the only curative treatment. Histological examination is important for the prognosis and follow-up plan.
ABSTRACT NO.: 209: Outcome analysis of congenital diaphragmatic hernia repair in neonates at a tertiary teaching institute: Category: Neonatal
ShelkeVinayakumarVinodYSShrishtiKVarshaTSamirkantANidhiSArchanaPAmitJGauravSDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Safdarjung Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: kumarvinaya1993@gmail.com
Aim: To study the factors affecting outcomes in neonates undergoing surgery for congenital diaphragmatic hernia (CDH).
Materials and Methods: Data of 125 neonates who underwent CDH repair over a period of 5 years, i.e., June 2017–June 2022 were reviewed retrospectively. Demographic data and clinical and laboratory parameters at admissions were recorded in a predesigned pro forma. Pre-operative, intra-operative, and postoperative adverse events were observed and recorded. The variables were subjected to test of significance and further multivariate analysis was done to assess the independent variables predicting the outcome.
Results: The overall mortality was 34/125 (27.2%). Gestational age, weight at admission, and associated congenital anomalies affect the postoperative outcome significantly. Preoperative primary pulmonary hypertension (PPHN), requirement of transfusion, inotropes, and respiratory support are significantly related to adverse postoperative mortality. Postoperative mortality and adverse events are higher in the neonates who require re-intubation, postoperative transfusion, surgical site infection, or ventilator-associated pneumonia.
Conclusion: Prematurity, low birth weight, associated congenital cardiac anomalies, and PPHN are associated with higher postoperative mortality. Birth weight, associated PPHN, and postoperative requirement of intubation are the independent risk factors for adverse postoperative outcome.
ABSTRACT NO.: 341: Postoperative outcomes of different surgical procedures in neonatal intestinal atresias at a tertiary care center: Category: Neonatal
PrasunaV SaiReddyP SarveshwaraDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Niloufer Hospital, Hyderabad, Telengana, India
E-mail: drprasunamogili@gmail.com
Objectives: The aim of this work was to assess the outcome for patients with jejunoileal atresia who underwent different surgical procedures such as resection and anastomosis and resection with different types of enterostomy.
Methods: During the period from May 2020 to May 2022, 108 neonates diagnosed to have intestinal atresia and who were operated for same in Niloufer Hospital, Hyderabad. Forty-eight patients were treated with primary resection/anastomosis, 60 patients underwent resection and stoma creation with 20 cases of Santulli's ileostomy, 24 cases of double-barrel ileostomy, and 16 cases end stoma. Presentation, treatment, and outcomes including mortality, morbidity, and nutrition status were reviewed and were compared among the four groups.
Results: Most of the babies presented early (54%) and prematurity (39%) was high among the babies who underwent resection and stoma with most of them having a double-barrel stoma. Highest mortality was seen with the resection and stoma group (32%) in view of uncontrolled sepsis and malnutrition compared to resection and anastomosis group (28%). Delayed transit was seen in few cases of resection and anastomosis group. Primary anastamosis group had highest reoperation rate (26%).
Conclusion: With modern advances in medical care, including parenteral nutrition and neonatal intensive care, the survival rate of neonates with jejunoileal atresia has improved dramatically. The complication rate of jejunoileal atresia still remains high due to the great disparity in the proximal and distal bowel, ineffective peristalsis, multiplicity of lesions, and associated peritonitis. Hence, the appropriate treatment for jejunoileal atresia remains controversial.
ABSTRACT NO.: 372: Factors predicting improving outcomes in gastroschisis: Category: Neonatal
ShahNirkhiJoshiRakeshRamjiJaishriVaghelaMaheshRataniAnizDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, B J Medical College, Ahmedabad, Gujarat, India
E-mail: meet.nirkhi@gmail.com
Introduction: There is a global disparity in the outcomes for gastroschisis, with more than 90% survival rates in the western world, while India and other low- or middle-income countries have only 30% survival rates. In our set up, we are seeing improved outcomes in the last 3 years.
Method: Data collection was done from September 2019 to June 2022. After admission and stabilization of the patient, they were taken for surgery as early as possible. After thorough peri-operative assessment, either primary closure or silo bag application was done. Postoperatively, aggressive fluid management and higher antibiotics along with ventilator care were provided.
Results: A total of 44 patients with gastroschisis were admitted at our institute during the study period. There were eight patients presenting within 6 h, 25 patients between 6 and 24 h and 11 presented after 1 day of life. One patient was delivered in our institute and all other were referred. Majority of patients were low birth weight (LBW) and presented with shock, hypothermia with edematous, and matted bowel and early onset septicemia.
Sixteen patients underwent initial silo bag application and later 1 underwent closure (we could not save others). Other 28 patients underwent repair of the defect with sheath closure in 15 patients and skin closure in 13 patients. Eleven patients survived and were discharged successfully. The improved survival in gastroschisis was seen in patients with early presentation and closure (7/8), term delivery (9/11), anatomical closure (11/11), institutional delivery at same center, prevention of sepsis before surgery (11/11), absence of matted edematous bowel (10/11), absence of other anomalies (11/11), management of sepsis with higher antibiotics, and enteral nutrition in small increments. The major morbidity was malabsorption (1/11) and failure to thrive (2/11) observed over an average period of 6 months.
Conclusion: Gastroschisis is a rare anomaly having higher mortality rates with late presentation, sepsis and edematous intestines being contributory factors. However, with early intervention, aggressive supportive management and prevention of sepsis, outcomes can be improved.
Free Paper Session - 4: Miscellaneous:ABSTRACT NO.: 33: Case series of retrieval challenges in pediatric distal airway foreign body: Category: Miscellaneous
AnandSuramyaJayaramHarishDebMainakDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Rainbow Children's Hospital, Hyderabad, Telangana, India
E-mail: suramya.anand@gmail.com
Aim: Retrieval of distally located tracheobronchial foreign bodies in children remains a challenge. This study aims to review the clinical presentation and management of such peripherally located foreign bodies with an emphasis on the various techniques utilized for successful bronchoscopic retrieval of the same.
Methods: All cases of foreign-body aspiration into the distal airway, i.e., airway beyond the main bronchi, presenting to the department of pediatric surgery in the time period between January 2015 and May 2022 were reviewed retrospectively. Demographic data and data on clinical presentation, type and location of foreign body inhaled, and techniques used for their retrieval were analyzed.
Results: Of 156 children who underwent bronchoscopic retrieval of foreign body, 24 children had foreign bodies that were located beyond the main bronchi going into the distal airways. Majority were males (M:F = 18:6) with most cases being in the 1–3 year age group (75%). Successful retrieval through rigid bronchoscopy was possible in all of these cases, although nontraditional instruments such as hysteroscopic biopsy forceps and stone grasper or fluoroscopic guidance were needed in 13 cases.
Conclusions: Foreign bodies located peripherally in the bronchial tree are a unique challenge. We emphasize the importance of choosing from a wide armamentarium of instruments available before resorting to open surgical retrieval.
ABSTRACT NO.: 111: Experience with tethered cord – Series of 30 cases: Category: Miscellaneous
GanguliAvikPatilRavindra Vora Sudhakar Jadhav SantoshDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, SJCKT's Pediatric Surgery Centre & PG Institute, Sangli, Maharashtra, India
E-mail: avikganguli001@gmail.com
Aim: Benefits of intervention before neurological deficit sets in.
Size: Series of 30 cases who underwent detethering. Two groups – (1) At the time of repair of spinal dysraphirsm (2) Post repair in childhood.
Methodology: Assessment – on clinical findings - signs of neurodeficit and on MRI findings. This is done pre- and postoperatively and at follow-up. Findings at operation – presence of lipomyelomeningocele, adhesion of cord or cauda equina to the dural sac, filum terminale adhered to the inferior end of dural sac, and the level of conus. The procedure of detethering is done accordingly. Procedures subtotal excision of lipoma, leaving alone the portion of dura attached to the cord at the time of closure of dura, division of filum terminale after asserting with nerve stimulator, excision of bony spur whenever necessary.
Follow-Up: Immediate complications such as leak or infections, clinical assessment of bowel and bladder, and neurological status early and late. Magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), nerve conduction studies, assessment of bowel, and bladder functions by doing special investigations whenever necessary.
Results: Early intervention, especially at the time of repair of spinal dysraphy is beneficial to prevent tethered cord syndrome. In cases presenting late especially with neural deficit, MRI is a must so that detethering surgery can be undertaken.
ABSTRACT NO.: 119: Tethered cord − Role of early detethering and mid- and long-term outcomes of detethering: Category: Miscellaneous
KumarNitinAlladiAnandDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College and Research Institution, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: nitinsbd@gmail.com
Aim: The aim of this study is to evaluate the (1) indicators for early detethering; (2) role of prophylactic detethering; and (3) mid- and long-term outcomes for detethering.
Materials: A retrospective study at department of pediatric surgery, BMCRI from January 2013 to till date. Follow-up ranging from 1 month to 9 years. All patients were evaluated with magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) spine with brain screening. Ultrasound KUB, urodynamic study, and muscle charting done in patients with neurodeficits.
Results: There were 102 patients: 58 male patients; 49 patients were infants; and 18 patients were diagnosed antenatal. Lipoma is the most common lesion (57) others in order of frequency diastematomyelia (20), dermal sinus (13), and low lying conus (12). Thirty-eight patients had neurological deficits; youngest patient with deficits was 2 month old. All underwent detethering (8 patient's required artificial dura for closure). Recovery was noted in 18/38 patients. Prophylactic detethering done in 64 patients. Cerebrospinal fluid leak was noted in 41 patients, one patient required exploration and closure of leak, others were managed with pressure bandage. 1 patient on follow-up had retethering underwent detethering. 3/38 patients with preoperative bowel and bladder deficits have undergone Mitrofanoff and mace.
Conclusion: Infants with tethered cord with cutaneous marker must undergo postnatal evaluation with MRI spine and brain screening to rule out Chiari malformation. Older children with neurodeficits require additional urological evaluation and muscle charting. Patients with lipo-meningocoele have early onset deficit and when operated early, recovery is good. Patients with dermal sinus must undergone detethering at diagnosis in view of increased incidence of arachnoiditis and meningitis. Patients with diastematomyelia require early detethering as ascent of spinal cord is prevented by bony/fibrous septa. In conclusion, we recommend that early detethering is indicated in patients with dermal sinus, diastematomyelia, and LipoMMC. Detethering is safe feasible with no adverse mid/long term sequalae especially in asymptomatic Patients and can be done by pediatric surgeon.
ABSTRACT NO.: 291: Tracheostomy-related complications and the effect of stoma maturation on the incidence of complications: Category: Miscellaneous
ReddyAbhishekCatherineChinnyNabeelAQZameerMMVinayCRaoSanjayD’CruzAshley JDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Narayana Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: abhishekreddy90@gmail.com
Introduction: Tracheostomy, while life-saving has significant complications. Inability to reposition a tracheostomy tube in a fresh tracheostomy is potentially life-threatening. Maturation of stoma is thought to decrease this risk.
Aims: To find the incidence of tracheostomy-related complications in children and study the impact of stoma maturation on these complications.
Methodology: This randomized controlled trials recruited all patients who had tracheostomies done by us, in our institute from April 01, 2017 to March 30, 2020. Patients were randomized to tracheostomy with or without maturation. Incidence of early (Inability to recannulate) and late (Granulation- tissue, tracheocutaneous fistula) was noted in both groups.
Results: Hundred and seventeen children were included. There were 41 girls and 76 boys. The median age at the time of tracheostomy was 10 months. The mean weight of patients was 10 kg. Fify six patients were randomized to stoma-maturation group and 61 to nonmaturation group. The average number of tracheostomy days was 176. There were three children (2.6% incidence) who had difficult re-cannulation after accidental decannulation, all of whom were in the nonmatured group. Overall incidence of granulation tissue formation was 21.4% (25 of 117); 14.8 % (9 of 61) in nonmatured group and 28.6% (16 of 56) in matured group (P: 0.041). Of the total 117 patients, 73 patients were decannulated and 40 children were followed till 6 months. The overall incidence of tracheocutaneous-fistula was 15%, 6.25% in nonmatured group, and 27.8% in matured-group (P - 0.1095). There was no tracheostomy-related mortality in this study.
Conclusion: Pediatric tracheostomy is a relatively safe procedure and can be done in all age groups. Maturing stomas decreases the incidence of difficult recannulation, especially in the early postoperative period. Although it is associated with an increased incidence of granulation tissue formation, it is also associated with an increased incidence of tracheocutaneous-fistula formation requiring additional procedure for closure. In children with critical airways, maturation of stoma is recommended.
ABSTRACT NO.: 21: Outcomes of partial calvariectomy and onlay periosteoplasty: Twenty-five years’ experience in the management of craniosynostosis from a tertiary care centre: Category: Miscellaneous
SinghApoorvBajpaiMinuAnandSachitDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: dr.singhapoorv@gmail.com
Objectives: To study the outcomes of partial calvariectomy and onlay periosteoplasty in children with craniosynostosis.
Methods: Single-center retrospective study. Children with craniosynostosis who had undergone surgery during the period from January 1993 to December 2017 were included. Preoperative evaluation included an X-ray skull and a noncontrast computed tomography scan of the skull. Skull growth was assessed by comparing head circumference at different time points. A minimum follow-up duration of 1-year postsurgery was mandatory for inclusion.
Results: A total of 78 (M:F = 2:1) children were included in the study. Abnormal shape of the skull and exorbitism was common complaints at presentation. The median (interquartile range) age at surgery was 14.5 months (8–30.75). Coronal (76%) followed by sagittal (44%) and metopic (35%) were the most common sutures involved. Thirty-five (45%) children had involvement of multiple sutures while 12 (15%) were associated with syndromic presentation. After 1 year of follow-up, new bone formation without any bony gap was appreciable in 84% of cases. The follow-up head circumference of the cohort was within 2SD from the mean in 60% of children.
Conclusions: Partial calvariectomy and onlay periosteoplasty is associated with good functional and cosmetic outcomes. Most cases showed a resumption of normal skull growth at the end of 1 year of follow-up.

SeshadriLNShankarGowriDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: lnseshadri@gmail.com
Introduction: Surgical site infections (SSIs), the most pertinent from the surgeon's perspective of healthcare-associated infections contributing to ¼th burden. Although WHO affirms that patients in low-resource settings are at higher risk for SSI, data available are sparse with no published studies on SSI in neonates and pediatric patients in India.
Aim: The aim of this study is to study the incidence, contributing factors, and consequences of SSI.
Methods: A prospective ongoing study of all consecutive children, including neonates who underwent surgery at our institution, a tertiary care pediatric referral center in South India between January 2018 and March 2022 (period – 4 year 3 months, 51 months) was carried out. The initial pilot year (2018) experience resulted in a standard institution protocol for patient preparation, antibiotic prophylaxis, SSI surveillance, and hospital infection control that has been implemented thereon. Outcomes analyzed were incidence of SSI, hospital stay, additional surgery, need for readmission, and mortality. Univariate and multivariate statistical analysis was carried out on the data collected.
Results: A total number of 15,690 patients underwent surgical operative procedures during the study period. Two hundred and forty-seven developed SSI with overall SSI rate was 1.58%. Factors associated are gastrointestinal procedures, emergency setting, prolonged duration, contaminated wounds, and in neonates and infants. SSI contributes to prolonged hospital stay, readmission, and mortality.
Conclusion: SSIs represent a significant burden of postoperative morbidity in children undergoing surgery globally. Focused analysis of SSI helped us in understanding the failures and reducing them. This study provides valuable data on SSI in neonates and pediatric patients in Southern India.

Few cases require special techniques and ingenuity to safely perform the procedure.
Free Paper Session - 5: MIS: ABSTRACT NO.: 10: Comparison of recurrence and complication rates following laparoscopic inguinal hernia repair among preterm versus full-term newborns: A systematic review and meta-analysis: Category: MIS
KulkarniKaushalPogorelicZenonKrižanacZvonimirSinghApoorvAnandSachitBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Science, New Delhi, India
E-mail: ktorus8591@gmail.com
Aim: Laparoscopic inguinal hernia repair (LHR) in children has been widely performed in the last decades, although it is still not sufficiently researched in preterm (PT) infants. This systematic review and meta-analysis compared the recurrence and complication rates following laparoscopic hernia repair among preterm (PT) versus full-term (FT) newborns.
Methods: Scientific databases (PubMed, EMBASE, Scopus, and Web of Science databases) were systematically searched for relevant articles. The following terms were used: (laparoscopic hernia repair) AND (PT). The inclusion criteria were all PT newborns with a unilateral or bilateral inguinal hernia who underwent LHR. The main outcomes were the incidence of recurrence of hernia and the proportion of children developing postoperative complications in comparison with FT newborns following LHR.
Results: The present meta-analysis included four comparative studies. Three studies had a retrospective study design while one was a prospective study. A total of 1702 children were included (PT n = 523, FT n = 1179). The incidence of hernia recurrence showed no significant difference between the PT versus FT groups (RR = 2.58, 95% confidence interval CI 0.89–7.47, P = 0.08). A significantly higher incidence of complications was observed in the PT group compared to the FT group (RR = 4.05, 95% CI 2.11–7.77, P < 0.0001). The PT group of newborns accounted for 81% and 72% of the major and minor complications. The major complications were either nonsurgical (i.e., severe respiratory distress requiring reintubation with prolonged ventilation (or high-frequency ventilation), seizures, and bradycardia), or surgical (i.e., hydroceles requiring operative intervention and umbilical port-site hernia).
Conclusions: LHR in PT infants is associated with similar recurrence rates as in FT infants. However, the incidence of complications is significantly higher in PT versus FT infants.
ABSTRACT NO.: 41: How easy is it to do a laparoscopic hepaticojejunostomy versus hepduodenostomy? Prelim experience: Category: MIS
HemachandranTejaswiTarunJKJSundeepMKDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamil Nadu, India
E-mail: tejaswihemachandran53@gmail.com
Background and Aims: HD and HJ are two options available for reconstruction in Choledochal cyst. HD is favored among MIS surgeons as it reported to be quicker, simpler, easier and has a similar complication profile. HJ is favored by others concerned about bile reflux gastritis. We present our preliminary experience in both procedures with a focus on technical details that could indicate an ease of performing the operation.
Methods: From Jan 2020 till date, we have performed 6 LHD and 1 LHJ. A retrospective review of the videos was performed with attention to the requirements for LHJ, additional time taken, and the factors relating to the ease of completing the HJ.
Results: The LHJ requires an EndoGIA stapler and an additional 12 mm port. The HJ took an additional 80 min for the creation of an intracorporeal stapled Roux limb and Jejunojejunostomy. HJ anastomosis took 75 min + 4 min mesentery closure versus the HD anastomosis: Average of 100 min (85–135). The Roux limb is relatively mobile and can be moved around to view the posterior layer of sutures and adjust the angles of suturing this contributes to the ease of the operation.
Conclusion: The LHJ requires endoscopic staplers and takes a bit longer to complete the Roux limb and JJ. However the HJ anastomosis seems to be easier to perform due to the relative mobility of the Roux limb vis-à-vis the duodenum.
ABSTRACT NO.: 55: Umbilicoscopy: Fewer and fewer ports in pediatric laparoscopy: Category: MIS
SinghApoorvBajpaiMinuAnandSachitDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: dr.singhapoorv@gmail.com
Objective: To share the departmental experience of using the umbilicus as a single port of access in Pediatric laparoscopy using the co-axial scope.
Methods: A prospective study on 76 (n = 76) cases using co-axial scope facilitated by the presence of an additional working channel by a single surgeon. Observation parameters included were demographic details, diagnosis, operative procedure, operative time, complications, results, and outcome.
Results: Mean age of patients was 6.5 years (range 1–14 years). Patients diagnosed with gall-stone disease (n = 30), acalculous cholecystitis (n = 1), acute appendicitis (n = 17), undescended testis (n = 18), and others (n = 10; foreign-body ingestion, ovarian cyst, and pancreatic pseudocyst) underwent laparoscopic cholecystectomy (n = 31), appendicectomy (n = 17), orchidopexy (n = 18), or other (n = 10) procedures, respectively. Umbilicoscopy using a single port and a co-axial scope was successfully used in the other cases.
Conclusions: Improved cosmesis, shorter hospital stay, lesser postoperative pain, and early return to activities justify the future trend toward this innovative single-site surgery. The surgeon's experience suggests a swift learning curve for a surgeon comfortable with conventional laparoscopy.
ABSTRACT NO.: 185: Robotics in children – Technical modifications required in children: Category: MIS
SinhaArvindRathodKirtikumarSaxenaRahulRodhaMahaveer SinghJayakumarTKKrishnamurthyShreyasAithalShrilakshmiVijAyushiBhattSomyaDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Jodhpur, Rajasthan, India
E-mail: drarvindsinha@gmail.com
Introduction: Robotic-assisted surgical procedures are increasingly becoming the rule especially in adults. Use of robot is prominent in many advanced centers. Robotic pediatric surgery is a field which is making rapid strides in the management of various surgical conditions in children. However, once incorporated, pediatric robotic procedures introduce its own unique set of challenges due to small patients’ subset and an acute lack of space in a small child.
Aim: To evaluate our initial experience with surgical robotics and to document the technical modifications which we incorporated to cater to our small patient cohort.
Methods: It is an observational study of the initial subset of 40 pediatric surgical procedures which were managed with robotic assistance. All children were <18 years of age and undergoing robotic-assisted surgery were included.
Results: We have included pyeloplasty (n = 14), ureteric reimplantation (n = 12), nephrectomy (n = 7), adrenalectomy (n = 1), fundoplication (n = 4), and choledochal cyst (n = 2). We required three conversions to laparoscopy/open and one case with inadvertent bowel injury. The age of the patients ranged from 7 months to 16 years (median = 4 years) and the operative time ranged from 74 min to 365 min (median = 126 min).
Discussion: We discuss our initial experience with regard to learning curve and the various technical modifications that we needed to incorporate owing to the lack of space especially in our smaller cohort of children. We also discuss the problems that we faced and the need for various modifications from the “accepted” norms of port placement, numbers, and spacing. Various modifications that we needed to incorporate especially for pediatric robotic surgery included

modifications of remote centering of ports
modifications to sites of ports
modifications to initial port placement
numbers of ports, spacing, or placement of ports
our intense dependence of assistant ports.

In our hands with our initial 40 cases of pediatric surgery incorporating various technical modifications, we have successfully managed increasingly complex cases in smaller and younger patients.
ABSTRACT NO.: 275: Learning curve of laparoscopioc anorectoplasty for anorectal malformation in children: Category: MIS
TiwariAbhishekAgrawalVikeshAcharyaHimanshuSisodiyaRajpal SinghSharmaDhananjayDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, NSCB Medical College, Jabalpur, Madhya Pradesh, India
E-mail: abhiti81@gmail.com
Aims: Little data are available to assess the learning curve for laparoscopic anorectoploasty in cases of anorectal malformation in children. The aim of this current study is to investigate the characteristics of the learning curve for laparoscopic anorectoploasty in cases of anorectal malformation in children.
Methods: A retrospectively collected database comprising all medical records of the first 50 consecutive patients undergoing a laparoscopic anorectoploasty for anorectal malformation by one individual surgeon trained in pediatric surgery for 3 years was studied. Baseline information and postoperative outcomes were collected, and then, learning curves were analyzed using the cumulative sum (CUSUM) method. Patients were divided into two groups including Group A and Group B according to the cutoff points of the learning curve. Intraoperative characteristics and short-term outcomes were compared between the two groups.
Results: CUSUM plots revealed that the cutoff point of the learning curve was 18 cases. Comparison of the operative time between the two groups revealed that the total operative time (120.71 ± 15.27, 55.28 ± 3.62 min, P < 0.001) was decreased significantly. The intraoperative bleeding (20.36 ± 7.46 vs. 10.43 ± 9.18, P = 0.977) was decreased significantly (P < 0.01), hospital stay (6.51 ± 1.12 vs. 4.54 ± 0.95, P = 0.910), and the postoperative complications did not differ significantly between the two groups.
Conclusions: The learning curve for the laparoscopic anorectoploasty in cases of anorectal malformation in children is 18 cases. This learning curve can be used as the basis for performance guidance during training in future.

Aim: To compare the outcomes of laparoscopic PIRS (percutaneous internal ring suturing) versus open inguinal herniotomy in female patients.
Methods: Between February 2022 and June 2022, 20 female pediatric patients with inguinal hernia were included. 10 underwent open inguinal herniotomy and 10 were taken up for laparoscopic PIRS. Laparoscopic percutaneous internal ring closure suturing (PIRS) is performed with an umbilical port and a puncture to the skin in the groin, with no inguinal incision.
Results: Out of the 10 laparoscopic PIRS patients, 7 had bilateral open internal inguinal ring, out of which 4 had their bilaterality diagnosed on table. Conclusion: Laparoscopic PIRS could become the preferred method of inguinal hernia surgery in female patients due to its negligible scar and advantage of diagnosing bilaterality of the hernia.
Sunday, 16th October 2022: Free Paper Session - 6: UROLOGY: ABSTRACT NO.: 56: Role of RAAS pathway gene polymorphisms in congenital uropathies: Category: Urology
BhardwajAnkurBajpaiMinuAnandSachitLuthraKalpanaPandeyAnjaliKumarAlokAll India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: ankur.sprout@gmail.com
Aims: To study the prevalence of gene polymorphisms of RAS pathway genes in children with congenital anomalies of kidney and urinary tract (CAKUT) and evaluate their role in outcome.
Methods: A cross sectional study was done in 250 children (<14 years) with CAKUT and 150 controls over a period of 3 years (2019–2021). 3 diseases - posterior urethral valve (PUV), vesico ureteric reflux and pelvic ureteric junction obstruction were selected. Polymorphism of 4 genes of RAAS pathway-AGT, AT2R, AT1R and ACE was assessed in blood samples of subjects. Polymorphism frequency was analyzed with respect to clinical and radiological outcomes. Patients were followed over a period of 1 year to evaluate the role of gene polymorphisms in disease progression.
Results: While comparing the polymorphism frequencies in cases and controls, we found that disease alleles of all four genes were over represented in the case group and significant association was seen with 2 genes- AT2R (P = 0.03) and AT1R (P = 0.02). Multivariate analysis showed that odds of getting CAKUT were higher with following genotypes- ACE DD (+0.5 times), AT2R (+0.4 times), AT1R AC (+1.6 times), and AGT CC (=0.6 times). Progressive deteriorators formed 22% of the cases, more seen in PUV patients. Subgroup analysis of progressive deteriorators showed that AT2R G allele and ACE DD allele increased the odds of progressive deterioration by 7 and 14 times, respectively. Cumulative effect of pathogenic alleles of different RAS genes showed that co-existence of DD alleles with other alleles had the most serious outcomes, thus raising the possibility of synergism like a “second hit.”
Conclusion: Almost 1/4th of children with CAKUT deteriorated despite getting the adequate treatment. We found significant association of ACE and AT2R gene polymorphisms with incidence and progression of congenital uropathies.
ABSTRACT NO.: 135: Uroflowmetry – An objective solution for the functional evaluation of urethroplasty: Category: Urology
RashmiDKhannaChetnaGautamVasuPatelRishabhDebnathPinaki RanjanKundalVijay KumarDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, ABVIMS and Dr. Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: rashmi.dotihalkar@gmail.com
Aims: The assessment of the results of hypospadias repair remains problematic, as there exists a significant difference between the judgement of patients and operating surgeons. In order to eliminate the observer bias, we aim to establish uroflowmetry as an objective tool for the functional assessment.
Methods: A prospective observational study was conducted during the period of November 2019 to February 2021 of all cases of Snodgrass hypospadias surgery. A total of 44 patients were included in the study and were subjected to Uroflowmetry in the preoperative and postoperative (6 weeks and 12 weeks) period. Parameters studied included: Maximum flow rate (Qmax), average flow rate (Qmean), total voided volume, voiding time, and type of curve. The preoperative uroflowmetry rates were compared with those of postoperative.
Results: Out of a total of 44 children, 26 patients had coronal hypospadias while 18 had sub-coronal hypospadias. The mean age at presentation was 6 years. Although some of the patients (n = 20) presented with plateau shaped curve, at 12-weeks post urethroplasty, all the patients (n = 44) were noted to have a bell-shaped curve during micturition, indicating a normal voiding pattern. Qmax at 6 weeks was noted to be reduced in 6 patients as compared with pre-op rates by an average of 20.1% (9.3%–27.7%) but at 12-weeks postoperative, all the patients (n = 44) were noted to have an overall increase in Qmax as compared to baseline Uroflowmetry rates. The mean increase was 23.9% (4.2%–43.1%). 2 patients were diagnosed with subclinical urethral stenosis.
Conclusion: Uroflowmetry is an efficient tool in the functional assessment of hypospadias surgery, objectively analysing the changes in pattern and rates. This optimizes follow up in the long-term.
ABSTRACT NO.: 140: Primary obstructive mega ureter: Diagnostic dilemma and management: Category: Urology
MamathaBAlladiAnandDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College and Research Institute, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: mamathanaveen3@gmail.com
Introduction: Primary obstructive megaureter (POM) is a developmental defect which can be physiological and resolve or can progress to be pathological needing intervention. There are diagnostic challenges and controversies in management with very few clear protocols.
Aim: To review the diagnostic challenges and management protocols of POM at our institute.
Materials and Methods: Between April 2013 and April 2022, 23 units of POM s were observed in 20 patients with a minimum follow up of more than 6 months. Megaureter due to primary reflux, secondary to urethral strictures, posterior urethral valve or due to anatomical variations like in duplex system, ureterocele and megaureter in ectopic ureters were excluded.
Results: Median age of diagnosis was 2 months, M:F ratio - 14:6, bilateral involvement was seen in 3 and unilateral involvement in 17 (L:10, R: 7). Of the 23 units, 6 units managed conservatively (2 had bilateral involvement). Seventeen units underwent intervention (1 bilateral and 15 unilateral). Indications for intervention were solitary functioning kidney in 1, Bilateral VUJO 1, drop in function -1, urosepsis with preserved function (EC> 40 % function)-2, Poor function (EC function <40%)- 8, double obstruction (at vescicoureteric junction [VUJ] and pelviureteric junction [PUJ]) in 3. Type of intervention 6 underwent a single stage procedure (reimplantation) and 11 were staged procedures where ureterosotomy was done first followed by reimplant later. Of the 11 staged procedures, 3 had double obstruction at VUJ and PUJ. MCU, EC renogram, MRU, cystoscopy, and RGP were done in all patients.
Conclusion: EC renogram, MRU, RGP, and laparoscopy all help in diagnosis and differentiating POM from other causes of megaureter and in decision-making. MRU and RGP are specifically useful to rule out double obstruction in megaureter. Management varies from conservative to surgical intervention either as a single or staged procedure. There is definitely a role for conservative management in megaureter provided indications for interventions are predetermined.
ABSTRACT NO.: 239: Long-term renal functions in patients of posterior urethral valves undergoing urinary diversion as initial treatment: Category: Urology
MahajanJKSolankiSahileshSamujhRamDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, PGIMER, Chandigarh, India
E-mail: jkmahajan@gmail.com
Aims: Diversion is required in high-risk patients of posterior urethral valves (PUV) to protect the upper tracts. This analysis aims to find out if this objective is fulfilled in the long term.
Methods: From 2000 to 2020, 274 patients were treated for PUV; of which 35 patients required diversion procedures (vesicostomy-28 and ureterostomy-7). Three patients died and remaining 32 patients (11.67%) were analyzed.
Results: Age at diversion ranged from 10 days to 6 years (mean 13.1 months). Indications for diversion were continuing urosepsis (25), urinary ascites (3), failure of creatinine to fall on catheter (30), poor feeding lethargy (1), and nonavailability of instrument (1). At the time of diversion, all 32 patients had creatinine >1 mg%. Vesicoureteric reflux (VUR) was present in 43 (67%) renal units (grade IV-V 35, I-II 8). With diversion, VUR resolved in 6 units with grade I-II (75%) and 5 units with grade IV-V (14%). Parenchymal disease was present in 56 renal units (87%) on dimercaptosuccinic acid scans. Thirty patients have undergone reversal of diversion. Mean age at undiversion was 5.2 years (2–10 years) and mean period of follow-up period after undiversion was 5.3 years (2–12 years). Poor cortical function was seen in 34 (75%) renal units at 1 year, 24 units (47%) at closure and 27 units (54%) at last follow-up. Nadir creatinine <1 mg% was seen in 75% patient at closure but only 25% maintained this value at follow-up. Upper tract dilatation had subsided and remained so after closure. High-grade VUR persisted in 45% (14) renal units.
Conclusions: Use of diversion in high-risk PUV postpone renal failure progression compared to ablation alone. Delayed deterioration after un-diversion is a possibility; hence, diversion should be seen as a temporizing measure only.
ABSTRACT NO.: 297: Adequacy of lymph node dissection in the management of Wilm's tumor: Category: Urology
PetersNitin JBeheraShashwatiSamujhRamMalikMuneer ATrehanAmitaGuptaKirtiYaddanapudiSandhyaDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, PGIMER, Chandigarh, India
E-mail: nitinjamespeters@yahoo.com
Aims: Lymph node sampling has been the most variable form of protocol deviation in the surgical management of WT. LN positivity has been correlated with the upgradation of the tumor stage as well as the addition of enhanced adjuvant therapy including radiotherapy as applicable. No clear-cut guidelines on LN sampling exist.
Methods: This was a single surgical unit retrospective and prospective case−control study on patients of WT who underwent a radical nephrectomy from January 2017 to June 2020. The adequacy of lymph node yield and lymph node density affecting the overall survival and relapse rate as compared to previously operated patients with WT were assessed. Other factors affecting the LN dissection and LN positivity such as tumor volume, histology, laterality and stages were evaluated primarily, while secondarily, the extent of LN sampling was correlated with the survival and recurrence. Dissection of the named LN stations was done.
Results: A total of 46 children diagnosed with WT met the inclusion criteria, where the majority 32 (61%) were males and the rest 38% were females. The mean age was 3.2 years (standard deviation = 0.5 years). More than seven lymph node retrieval was possible in 32 patients (61.11%) out of 46. Five out of the other 25 patients in the cohort (13.04%) with positive LNs were found to possess larger tumor volume preoperatively (>750 cc). All the five patients with positive nodes had more than 7 lymph nodes sampled. The average number of LNs sampled was found to be significantly higher in patients of the 2nd half of the study with P < 0.001. Patients with more than 7 LNs sampled had a slightly higher median survival time.
Conclusion: The cohort of patients with LN retrieval (at least 7 or more) keeping in view the stations involved with respect to the tumor size and laterality showed to have higher median survival time and disease-free survival. However, larger studies with longer follow-up period are required to validate our results.
ABSTRACT NO.: 346: Inflammatory response after open and laparoscopic pyeloplasty in children: Category: Urology
NandanRuchiraUpadhyayayArj DeoPandeyVaibhavKumarDeepakDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Banaras Hindu University, Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: ruchira.nandan@gmail.com
Introduction: Laparoscopy has been thought to induce a less inflammatory response than laparotomy due to less trauma. Since, no study has been done in the Indian population, to describe the inflammatory response after open and laparoscopic pyeloplasty for pelvi-ureteric junction obstruction (PUJO) in children, we studied the above.
Methods: A prospective study was done to include patients of PUJO older than 3 years who underwent pyeloplasty. They were randomly divided into the open (OP) and laparoscopic (LP) pyeloplasty groups. The inflammatory response was assessed by measuring interleukin-6 (IL-6) and IL-10 levels in both OP and LP groups. The median IL-6 level, IL-10 level, and IL-6/IL-10 ratio were compared between OP and LP groups, just before surgery, after 30 min of surgery, immediate postop, and 48 hours after surgery.
Results: A total of 31 patients in OP and 26 patients in the LP group were included. The median IL-6 level was high in LP than OP group, after 30 min of surgery (P =0.001) and immediate postoperative (P = 0.030). After 48 h of surgery, it was high in the OP than LP group (P = 0.012). The median IL-10 level was high in LP than OP group after 30 min of surgery (P < 0.001). It was high in OP than LP group in immediate postoperative (P < 0.001) and after 48 h of surgery (P = 0.017). The median IL-6/IL-10 level ratio was significantly high in LP than OP group in the immediate postoperative period (P < 0.001).
Conclusion: LP has markedly less surgical stress and early recovery from the perioperative stress compared to the open approach. The minimal tissue injury caused by the laparoscopic approach results in a smaller and shorter acute phase response.
ABSTRACT NO.: 321: Vesicoscopic ureteric re-implantation: Our experience and lessons learnt: Category: Urology
RaghavendranDivyaRobertAntonyDashVedarthChoudhuryShounakVenkateshwaranLathaChatterjeeArkaKindersurge Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: divya.m57@gmail.com
Aim: The aim of this study is to describe our early experience with vesicoscopic ureteric reimplantations and the lessons learned while incorporating this technique.
Methods: Between May 2017 and June 2022, 45 patients underwent vesicoscopic reimplantation at a tertiary care center for vesicoureteric reflux (VUR) (greater than grade 3) and vesicoureteric junction obstruction/paraureteric diverticulum. The study examines the early and late outcomes, complications, and lessons learnt.
Results: Out of the 45 patients who underwent vesicoscopic reimplantation, 5 were converted to open reimplantation. The average duration of stay was 4.5 days for those who underwent vesicoscopic reimplantation, whereas it was 6 days for those who underwent open surgery. 40 patients underwent reimplantation for VUR and five for other causes (VUJO, Megaureter). Challenges were faced in the early part of the learning curve. Challenges included those with port insertion, in the initial stages, conversion to open surgery was done due to the instability of the ports as reinsertion was difficult. Dissection of ureters initially difficult was resolved by using a combination of blunt and sharp dissection. Insertion of the Double J ureteric stent was time-consuming earlier which was sorted out by using the outer sheath of the suction catheter to guide its insertion.
Conclusion: Vesicoscopic ureteric reimplantation is likely to remain the preferred method of this surgery. Even though it has a relatively steep learning curve, the advantages highly justify the use of this technique.
Free Paper Session - 7: HEPATO BILIARY: ABSTRACT NO.: 25: Biliary dyskinesia: Are we managing it correctly in the pediatric population?: Category: Hepato Biliary
SinhaAnkitMattsonAnjaNjereIkeSinhaCKDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Lister Hospital, Stevenage, Hertfordshire, UK
E-mail: cksinha11@gmail.com
Aims: To find out the correct approach to the management of biliary dyskinesia in children.
Methods: Literature was reviewed using MeSH terms to find studies detailing pediatric laparoscopic cholecystectomy (LC) at pediatric centers (PCs) and adult centers (ACs) that included biliary dyskinesia. Statistical analysis was performed using Stata-16 (Stata Corp, USA).
Results: A total of 11,368 pediatric patients from 31 papers between 2000 and 2020 were found. They met the inclusion criteria and mentioned biliary dyskinesia as an indication for LC. Of this, 81% (n = 9165) were performed at PCs and 19% (n = 2203) at ACs. Cholelithiasis was the most common indication for LC at both, with a rate of 69% at PCs and 31% at ACs (P < 0.0001). The second most common indication for LC was biliary dyskinesia (29% [ACs] and 17% [PCs] [P < 0.0001]). Nonspecific cholecystitis was the commonest presentation, presenting as 16% of cases at PCs and 39% at ACS (P < 0.0001). Acute cholecystitis requiring LC was also higher at ACs than PCs (12% vs. 5.7% respectively, P < 0.0001). Chronic cholecystitis presented as 16% of cases at PCs and 29% at ACs (P < 0.0001).
Conclusions: Biliary dyskinesia was managed operatively by LC significantly more frequently at ACs. At PCs, a more conservative approach to treatment was adopted, which reflects a different approach to the management of biliary dyskinesia by the pediatric surgeons in comparison to the adult surgeons. After cholelithiasis, biliary dyskinesia was found to be the second most common indication for LC, which reflects either the rising incidence or rising rate of diagnosis.
ABSTRACT NO.: 158: Iatrogenic extrahepatic biliary tract injuries - diagnostic and management challenge: Category: Hepato biliary
KhannaChetnaDebnathPinaki RanjanKundalVijayMeenaAtul KumarShahShaluSenAmitaDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, ABVIMS and DR RML Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: drpinaki_mch@yahoo.co.in
Aim: Biliary surgery in children has always been a challenge to the treating surgeon and at times very difficult, although meticulously planned management may produce satisfactory results. We are sharing our experience of treating complicated redo biliary surgeries at our tertiary institute with multidisciplinary approach and good outcome.
Methods: A retrospective, observational study was conducted from February 2017 to February 2022. We have treated 13 complicated re-do Biliary surgeries referred to us from different hospitals over a period of 5 years.
Results: Out of these 13 cases, 5 were having post laparoscopy cholecystectomy complications with severe biliary peritonitis, 4 were having complicated choledochal cyst with biliary peritonitis, one patient had complete ligation of common bile duct (CBD) during open cholecystectomy and three had continuous bile leak with biliary peritonitis following intervention of liver abscesses. The common factors included biliary peritonitis and have referred to us from other hospitals. The age ranged between 1.5 years and 14 years and sex ratio 7 female patients and 6 male patients. All had successful outcome after re-do surgery in the form of hepaticojejunostomy for all choledochal cyst patient, CBD injured cholecystectomy patients and biliary stenting followed by laparotomy for all liver abscess pts after initial stabilization and treating sepsis.
Conclusion: Re-do biliary surgery in children following unsuccessful attempts, at times is very difficult situation to manage. The main culprit here is biliary peritonitis and sepsis which makes the situation worse. The initial management is treating the sepsis with endoscopic intervention for safe passage of bile to duodenum and letting out the intraperitoneal bile. Later thorough radiological investigations and successful planning for definitive surgery with execution yields satisfactory results. We suggest a multi-disciplinary approach for the diagnosis and treatment of re-do complicated biliary surgery in children. Surgery may be indicated according to the patient's clinical condition, radiological findings and failure of non-operative treatment.
ABSTRACT NO.: 177: Acute pancreatitis: A clinico-epidemiological profile: Category: Hepato Biliary
ManasaABabuNarendraDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute Of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: manasa.ananda@gmail.com
Aim: To study the clinical presentation, management, and outcomes of acute pancreatitis in a pediatric age group.
Materials and Methods: All children diagnosed to have pancreatitis between 2014 and 2020 were included. Data on imaging techniques, biochemical profile, management and duration of stay was collected and analyzed.
Results: We managed 133 children with an equal gender distribution. Majority were between 5 and 10 years. Among the 122 with the first episode, pain was the commonest symptom (74%), with an average duration of 4.17 days. Ultrasonogram (USG) was the primary modality of investigation with a sensitivity of 94%. Select patients with suspicion of underlying conditions or complications underwent computed tomography (CT) scan (n-26 [23%]) and magnetic resonance cholangiopancreatography (MRCP) (n - 11 [9%]). 69.6% of the children had elevated amylase and 68% had elevated lipase at admission. Management protocol included hydration, pain control with 48–72 h of gut rest. 90% had a mild and self-limiting disease with an average of 5.72 days stay at the hospital. Twelve (10%) children underwent intervention with an average duration of 20.5 days stay at the hospital. Twenty-one (15%) children had recurrent episodes of pancreatitis with 3 children having >2 episodes. All children underwent USG with more than 50% undergoing CT/MRCP in search for an underlying etiology. Twenty children were conservatively managed in contrast to one (5%) who underwent pig tail insertion. Duration of hospital stay was 4.8 days. 22% children were diagnosed with a definitive etiology (cholelithiasis- common) in the acute phase, 14% (divisum-common) during the recurrent episode.
Conclusion: Acute pancreatitis constitutes a significant disease burden in children (20–25 patients/year). Majority have an uneventful and a quick recovery. Children with recurrent pancreatitis had a good short term outcome. Majority (78%) are not diagnosed with a specific etiology and this group highlights the need for genetic analysis, which is currently limited due to the cost constraints.
ABSTRACT NO.: 191: Single-port single-instrument laparoscopic cholecystectomy: Our experience: Category: Hepato Biliary
KrishnanNellaiBhardwajAnkurGoelPrabudhYadavDevendra KumarJainVisheshDhuaAnjan KumarAnandSachitBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
