Aims: To present our experience with the technique of single-port single-instrument laparoscopic cholecystectomy for cholelithiasis and evaluate the learning curve of a single surgeon.
Methods: Prospective study on consecutive children who underwent single-port single-instrument laparoscopic cholecystectomy for uncomplicated gall-stone disease over 6-years. Data collected included operating time, blood loss, intra-or-post-operative complications, time to resume soral feeding, duration of hospital stay and final outcomes.
Results: Thirty-nine children (male:female = 20:19) were included. Mean age at surgery was 10.3 years. Dividing the cohort into two groups of initial 19 and later 20, the mean operating time was observed to decrease from 80 to 72 min. There was minimal blood loss (not more than 20 ml in any case). Time to oral feeds decreased from 32 to 24 h. Duration of hospital stay decreased from 3 days to 2 days. One patient in the latter group developed a bile leak (presented one week after discharge with pain abdomen) was managed conservatively with pigtail drainage.
Conclusion: Single-port single-instrument laparoscopic cholecystectomy is a simple, easy to learn, cosmetically acceptable technique of cholecystectomy which has a short learning curve and excellent result.
ABSTRACT NO.: 183: Hepatic perfusion index – A novel noninvasive method of assessing success of dean warren shunt in cases of extrahepatic portal venous obstruction: Category: Hepato Biliary
GoswamiChandramouliGoelPrabudhBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: chandramouligoswami1234@gmail.com
Aim: To assess success of Dean Warren Shunt in improving Hepatic Perfusion in Extra Hepatic Portal Venous Obstruction (EHPVO) by measuring Hepatic Perfusion Index (HPI).
Methods: Prospective study upon 18 (n = 18) patients of EHPVO including preoperative (Group I; n = 9) and post spleen-preserving distal lienorenal shunt (Group II; n = 9). A radionucleotide angiography evaluation with Tc99m -mebrofenin, bromo- 2,4,6-trimethyl acetanilide iminodiacetic acid was performed upon all patients included. Region of interest curves were drawn from images captured and time-activity curves generated thereof to calculate the hepatic and portal flow.
Results: Mean age of patients was 7.6 years (Group I: 8.7 years; Group II: 6.9 years). Preoperative clinical profile was similar between the two groups. Mean duration after surgery in Group II was 2.4 years (9 months – 4 years). Prior to inclusion, patency of shunt in all patients in Group II was demonstrated by a Doppler study or CT Portography (where Doppler was not able to confirm the patency satisfactorily). HPI was significantly higher in pre-operative cases of EHPVO (median of 53.9) as compared to cases post DSRS (median 27.6).
Conclusion: HPI is raised in cases with EHPVO which is reduced after the spleen-preserving distal lienorenal shunt. Improvement in hepatic perfusion after shunt surgery is indicative of selective shunt.
ABSTRACT NO.: 38: Spontaneous perforation of choledochal cysts: Clinical implications and management: Category: Hepato Biliary
TejaswiHAvinashKumarArun.JehangirSusanDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Christian Medical College, Vellore, Tamil Nadu, India
E-mail: tejaswihemachandran53@gmail.com
Background and Aims: Spontaneous perforation of choledochal cyst (CC) is a relatively rare condition with incidence ranging from 1% to 7%. The predisposing factors are not well established. There is also no consensus regarding the ideal management. Primary drainage followed by delayed excision was originally recommended but there is recent evidence supporting a single stage primary repair. We aim to evaluate the clinical characteristics of perforated CC and compare it with non-perforated CC to aid in determining the risk factors and optimum line of management.
Materials and Methods: Retrospective review of CC treated in Christian Medical College, Vellore over a period of 10 years (2011–2020) was done.
Results: A total of 350 CC were operated with six of those patients having spontaneous perforation - 1.7%. Mean age of children with perforated cysts was 26 months consisting of four boys and two girls. Abdominal pain, jaundice, and fever were the common clinical presentations. Imaging revealed common bile duct diameter ranging from 4 to 10.5 mm. In all six patients either the perforation or the diagnosis of CC was missed preoperatively. Intra-operative perforations identified were either contained or sealed off with two children having loculated biliary ascites. The site of perforation varied from proximal common hepatic to distal common bile duct. All patients underwent primary repair and are doing well on follow-up.
Conclusion: Preoperative diagnosis of perforated CC is a challenge. Single-stage cyst excision with biliary reconstruction is possible. These reports aid in developing an optimal treatment plan for such rare conditions.
ABSTRACT NO.: 43: Hepatic resection in children: Initial experience: Category: Hepato Biliary
KumarBasantKennagantiPujanaYadavRajanikantSinghRKKumarRahulUpadhyayaVDDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, SGPGIMS, Lucknow, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: drbasant189@gmail.com
Background: Hepatic resection in children has limited indications and mostly performed due to liver tumors (0.5%–2% of all pediatric tumors). It needs extensive pre- and intra-operative planning for favorable outcome. Here, we present our initial experience.
Methodology: We retrospectively reviewed patients underwent liver resections from December 2017 to May 2022. Data analyzed were pertaining to the age, sex, presentation, pre/post-text stage, diagnostic evaluation performed, management, and follow-up results. The final outcomes were assessed.
Results: Eighteen patients were included in this cohort. Diagnosis was hepatoblastoma (11), undifferentiated embryonal sarcoma (2), malignant epithelioid hemangioendothelioma (1), mesenchymal hamartoma (2), and adenoma (2). Seven patients were female and age ranged from 8 month to 13 years (Median 3 years). Hepatectomy was divided into two groups: (a) minor resections- <3 segment (5 patients) and (b) major resections- >3 segments removed (12 patients) or mesohepatectomy (2 patients) and operative results were analyzed. Total operative time ranged from 110 to 400 min and blood losses ranged from 50 to 300 ml. Postop morbidities were surgical site infection (3) and chest infection (2). Chemotherapy was given in 13 patients. One patient with hemangioendotheliomas was died due to local recurrence and metastasis. Rest 17 patients are under follow-up without local or remote recurrence. Follow-up ranged from 6 to 54 months.
Conclusion: Management of liver tumors requires multidisciplinary team approach. Major and minor hepatectomy has no significant difference in morbidity. Careful evaluation, planning, and early appropriate chemotherapy are necessary. No hesitation to ask for help if required.
Free Paper Session - 8: Thoracic: ABSTRACT NO.: 14: Impact of preservation of the azygos vein during surgical repair of esophageal atresia-tracheoesophageal fistula: Category: Thoracic
SinghApoorvKainthDeepikaAnandSachitBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: dr.singhapoorv@gmail.com
Objective: The study aims to compare the surgery outcomes in newborns of esophageal atresia-tracheoesophageal fistula (EA-TEF) undergoing repair of EA-TEF with and without azygos vein preservation in terms of its complications.
Methods: The authors systematically searched the databases PubMed, EMBASE, Web of Science, and Scopus through December 2020. The incidence of anastomotic complications and chest infection was compared among the two groups of newborns, i.e., those undergoing surgical repair with (group A) and without azygos vein preservation (group B). Statistical analysis was performed using a fixed-effects model, and pooled risk ratio (RR) and heterogeneity (I2) were calculated. The methodological quality of the studies was assessed using the Downs and Black scale.
Results: Six comparative studies, consisting of a total of 671 newborns, were included in the meta-analysis. Compared to group B, newborns belonging to group A showed a significantly lower incidence of pneumonitis in the postoperative period (relative risk [RR] 0.31; 95% confidence interval [CI] 0.17–0.57, P = 0.0001). However, no significant difference in the incidence of anastomotic complications, including anastomotic leak (RR 0.73; 95% CI 0.48–1.12, P = 0.15) and stricture (RR 0.63; 95% CI 0.36–1.09, P = 0.10) was observed between the two groups. The average Downs and Black scale scores ranged from 20 to 24. The risk of bias was low (n = 1) and moderate (n = 5) in the included studies. Kappa statistics showed a value of 0.902 (P < 0.001), highlighting an almost perfect agreement between the two observers.
Conclusion: The present meta-analysis revealed the superiority of surgical repair of EA-TEF performed with preservation of azygos vein in terms of the incidence of postoperative chest infection. However, no significant difference in the occurrence of anastomotic leak and stricture was observed between the two groups.
ABSTRACT NO.: 79: Whether prophylactic intraoperative chest drain insertion in esophageal atresia-tracheoesophageal fistula is an evidence-based practice or just a prejudice: A systematic review and meta-analysis: Category: Thoracic
SehgalMehakAnandSachitSinghApoorvBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: mehak.sehgal91@gmail.com
Aim: Various controversial practices in the management of esophageal atresia-tracheoesophageal fistula can be noticed among pediatric surgeons and routine intraoperative chest drain (IOCD) insertion is often debated. This meta-analysis aims to compare the post- operative outcomes among neonates with and without IOCD insertion.
Methods: The authors searched EMBASE, PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science on April 30, 2021. The requirement for chest-drain in the postoperative period (POCD), anastomotic leak, mortality rate, and revisit(s) to the operation room (RVOR) were compared among two groups of newborns, i.e. groups A and B with and without IOCD insertion respectively. The statistical analysis was performed using a fixed-effects model. The pooled risk ratio (RR) and heterogeneity (I2) were calculated. The methodological quality of the studies was assessed utilizing the Downs and Black scale.
Results: A total of 498 neonates were included in the present analysis. As compared to group B, new-borns within Group A showed no significant difference in the requirement for POCD (RR 2.47; 95% confidence interval [CI] 0.88–6.98, P = 0.09), the occurrence of anastomotic leak and/or pneumothorax (RR 1.35; 95% CI 0.89–2.06, P = 0.16), and mortality rate (RR 2.24; 95% CI 0.81–6.26, P = 0.12). However, RVOR was significantly higher in Group A (RR 1.75; 95% CI 1.07–2.87, P = 0.03). All included studies had a moderate risk of bias.
Conclusions: The present meta-analysis revealed no additional benefit of prophylactic IOCD insertion. However, due to moderate risk of bias, further studies are needed for an optimal comparison between the two groups.
ABSTRACT NO.: 96: Unusual airway foreign bodies in children - demographics and management: Category: Thoracic
BoseSumonaShaliniHRajuRajkiranKiranMKumarAR PrasannaShubhaAMDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, St. Johns Medical College, Hospital, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: sumonabose06@gmail.com
Aim: To study the demographics and management of unusual airway foreign bodies in children.
Materials and Methods: A retrospective observational study (2000–2020) of all children with unusual airway foreign bodies, who underwent bronchoscopic removal at a tertiary care hospital. The demographic details, types of foreign bodies, clinical and radiological features, management, and outcomes were collated and analyzed. Common foreign bodies such as nuts and seeds and vegetable aspirations were excluded.
Results: A total of 531 children had bronchoscopic retrieval of airway foreign bodies. 74 (59 males/15 females) among them had unusual foreign bodies. These ranged from pen caps, whistles, plastic objects, pins to coal piece, stones, clay, pencil, gold ornament, and glass bangle. 31 children presented within 1 day of aspiration, 37 within 1 month and 6 had delayed presentation upto 6 months. 39 were <5 years, the rest were aged from 5 to 15 years. Cough, respiratory distress, fever, choking and noisy breathing were the common presentations. Stridor, whistling, cyanosis, loss and change of voice were other symptoms. CXR showed ipsilateral hyperinflation commonly (23), haziness (21), radio-opaque foreign body (17) and was normal in 14. Left main bronchus followed by right main bronchus and trachea was the sites of impaction. Four children required additional procedures such as tracheotomy and thoracotomy. There was only one mortality in the study cohort.
Conclusion: Aspiration of unusual foreign bodies is not uncommon in children. Most aspirations are witnessed and predominant in boys. Left bronchus is the common site of impaction and X-ray clinches the diagnosis. Presentation is early and is amenable to bronchoscopic retrieval with favorable outcome.
ABSTRACT NO.: 114: Relevance of various prognostic classifications in oesophageal atresia-tracheo-oesophageal fistula: Category: Thoracic
DjendovFlorinNjereIkeSaeedAtifMukhtarZahidSinhaCKDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, St. George's Hospital, London, UK
E-mail: florin.djendov@yahoo.com
Aim: Survival for neonates born with esophageal atresia (OA) and tracheo-oesophageal fistula (TOF) has improved significantly in the recent past. With this background, we aimed to look at the survival rates in OA-TOF babies as per various published prognostic-classifications.
Methods: Records of neonates managed for OA-TOF in our center, between 2010 and 2021 (12 years) were reviewed. Data on gestational age, birth weight, anatomical types, associated anomalies, postoperative course, and survival outcomes were collected and analyzed with respect to various published classifications.
Results: Among 68 neonates, mean birthweight was 2500 g (range 535–3860 g), with most of them (67.6%) being born at term (>37 weeks). Associated anomalies were identified in 37 patients, of whom 21 (56.7%) had cardiac anomalies, while VACTERL association was present in 15 (40.5%). The stratification according to the high-risk prognostic groups included three patients (4.4 %) in each Spitz III and Sinha B; 4 patients (5.8%) in Bremen A/B III; 14 patients (20.5%) in Montreal II, and 19 patients (27.9%) in Waterston C. Survival rates were: 95% in Sinha A and 66.7 % in Sinha B (p=0.16). Similarly, in Spitz I and II combined-group survival was 95% and in Spitz III survival was 66.7%. In both Sinha A and Spitz (I and II combined), two neonates died due to Trisomy 18. In Sinha B and Spitz III, one death was due to intraventricular hemorrhage (IVH). These babies could not reach the operation room. Outcomes for other classifications were comparable to Spitz and Sinha classifications.
Conclusion: Associated major cardiac anomalies and low birth weight are relevant prognostic factors affecting outcomes for neonates with OA-TOF. However, with improvement in neonatal care and survival rates, accuracy of prognostic classifications has become skeptical. Other serious factors like chromosomal anomalies and IVH are still major causes of death in these babies.
ABSTRACT NO.: 127: Comparative study of different modality of treatment and its outcome for empyema thoracis: Category: Thoracic
AnandAbhishekBanerjeeTJBasuAKGuptaPBhaumikKDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Institute of Child Health, Kolkata, West Bengal, India
E-mail: Pandeyabhi16@gmail.com
Aim: To discuss about different modality of treatment of empyema thoracis with respect to short and long term morbidity, duration of hospital stay, and expenses.
Materials and Methods: The study included 50 children with a mean age of 36 months. Patients with loculated effusion and pleural tap suggestive of exudate were selected. Each patient fulfilling the inclusion criteria were sequentially allotted to the four group of intervention offered as primary modality of treatment as followed- for each four patient coming consecutively,

First one - Only drain
Second one - Drain and streptokinase
Third one - Primary VATS
Fourth one - Thoracotomy, decortication, and drainage.

This was irrespective of duration of the illness. If any primary modality failed in a particular case, it was managed by alternative modality as per standard practice guidelines.
Results: Five patients improved with ICD. Other 8 cases did not improve and treated with VATS and thoracotomy. In 12 patients, primary VATS was performed and 3 patients underwent VATS after streptokinase therapy, and in two patients, VATS was converted to open procedure. Patients who underwent VATS as a primary treatment modality had less hospital stay and hospital expense (9 days and Rs. 53720). All patients discharged in healthy condition.
Conclusion: In patients with loculated, complex fibrino-purulent parapneumonic empyema thoracis, a primary treatment strategy of VATS is associated with shorter hospital stay, lesser morbidity, and less cost than other treatment strategy, irrespective of days of illness at primary intervention. Thoracotomy is often the last resort for rescue in case of failure of other less invasive interventions.
ABSTRACT NO.: 258: Should retrosternal re-routing to be considered over classical transhiatal route in esophageal substitutions for pure esophageal atresia-tracheoesophageal fistula diversions: Category: Thoracic
RaniKritikaJadhavVinayShankarGowriDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: kritika.descent@gmail.com
Background and Aim: Classical transhiatal route for esophageal replacement in esophageal atresia-tracheoesophageal fistula (EA/TEF) diversion has been considered the standard approach. However, this approach has been associated with intraoperative and immediate postoperative challenges.
The aim of this study is

To compare transhiatal with substernal rerouting of esophageal replacement in pure EA/TEF diversions
To compare immediate and short-term outcomes of TH with substernal approach.

Methods:

We reviewed esophageal replacements carried out in our institute between August 2015 and June 2022. A total of 18 patients (5 girls and 13 boys) with ages ranging from 11 to 15 years
The indications were EA with TEF in 11/18 patients and caustic burn in 7/18 cases
Esophageal replacement was performed through an abdominal midline incision by one of three methods, namely: colon transposition in 2 and isoperistaltic gastric tube in 16 cases
The preferred approaches were classical transhiatal in 14 pts and retrosternal in 4 pts.

Results: Eighteen children underwent esophageal replacements, 11 were for pure EA/TEF diversions, and 7 were for caustic esophageal injury. 7/11 (66%) had transhiatal approach and 4/11 (36%) had retrosternal approach for conduit re-routing in pure EA/TEF. All the caustic injury cases had esophagectomy with replacements through transhiatal route.
4/7 cases of pure EA/TEF where TH route were used expired in immediate postoperative period due to sudden cardiac arrest and remaining 3/7 had prolonged ICU stay for cardiac arrhythmias, pulmonary edema, cardiac instability, ventilator dependency, and prolonged inotropes/milrinone supports.
In comparison, 4/7 cases in whom substernal route were employed for esophageal replacements, children had a smooth postoperative recovery, no mortality with no cardiac instability noted. All these children were extubated on pod 3, minimal ICU stay and oral feeds started on pod 6.
Conclusion: High morbidity and mortality were seen in classical TH approaches in pure EA/TEF children and required prolonged ICU care compared to children in whom sub sternal routes were employed. In our experience, substernal approach had excellent result compared to classical TH approach in immediate postoperative period in pure EA/TEF diversion cases.
ABSTRACT NO.: 313: Thoracoscopic diaphragmatic hernia repair in newborns – Importance of selection criteria: Category: Thoracic
NaazAishaPrabhakaranAswinGopiAdharshShajanAntonyAbrahamMohanVishwanathNaveenBinduSDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Amrita Institute of Medical Sciences, Kochi, Kerala, India
E-mail: aishanaaz17@gmail.com
Aim: To define selection criteria for thoracoscopic repair of congenital diaphragmatic hernia in newborns for better outcome.
Materials and Methods: This was a retrospective study. Newborn cases who underwent thoracoscopic diaphragmatic hernia repair at Amrita Institute of Medical Sciences between year 2014 and 2021 were studied.
Results: Total 68 babies underwent thoracoscopic diaphragmatic hernia repair out of which there13 babies required mesh repairs. Only 2 cases had recurrences which underwent open repair. There were two deaths and one conversion to open repair, but as the selection criteria became more defined the outcome improved. These selection criteria include mode of ventilation, percentage of FiO2 requirement, mean airway pressures, inotrope requirement, primary pulmonary hypertension, tolerance of lateral position, gestation age, and birth weight. Side of the defect alone did not affect the outcome if other criteria were considered.
Conclusion: Selection criteria are important for benefitting babies with congenital diaphragmatic hernia with better outcome with less invasive thoracoscopic repair.
Key words: Thoracoscopic, diaphragmatic hernia, selection criteria
Free Paper Session - 9: EXPERIMENTAL: ABSTRACT NO.: 64: Comparison of outcomes of different methods of bowel preparation for elective colorectal surgeries: Category: Experimental
MoitraKrishnenduBhaumikKuntalBasuAshoke KumarDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Institute of Child Health, Kolkata, West Bengal, India
E-mail: krishnendumoitra@gmail.com
Aim: To compare the different methods of bowel preparation for elective colorectal surgeries in terms of postoperative electrolyte imbalance in pediatric age group.
Methods: One hundred and forty-four patients from 1 year to 12 years of age posted for elective colorectal surgeries are randomly allocated into two groups. Group “A” was assigned bowel preparation using per rectal/per stomal wash using warm normal saline and Group “B” was assigned bowel preparation using oral polyethylene glycol solution per orally. Outcomes are compared by determining the serum electrolyte levels of sodium and potassium after 12 h, 24 h, and 48 h after surgery.
Results: In Group “A,” out of 72 patients, 18% (n = 13) patients had both postoperative hyponatremia and hypokalemia and 2.7% (n = 2) patients had hyponatremia only. 38.8% (n = 28) patients from Group A had postoperative hypokalemia and 40.2% (n = 29) patients had no electrolyte imbalance. In Group “B,” 22.2% (n = 16) patients had both postoperative hyponatremia and hypokalemia and 12.5% (n = 9) patients had hyponatremia only. 58.3% (n = 42) patients from Group “B” had postoperative hypokalemia and 6.9% (n = 5) patients had no electrolyte imbalance.
Conclusion: Bowel preparation for elective colorectal surgeries using per rectal/per stomal wash using warm normal saline is more safer in terms of postoperative serum electrolyte imbalance as compared to bowel preparation using oral polyethylene glycol solution only.
ABSTRACT NO.: 106: The role of laser tissue welding in pediatric surgery: Category: Experimental
JawaleSagarDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Jawale Institute of Pediatric Surgery, Sangli, Maharashtra, India
E-mail: drsagarjawale@gmail.com
Introduction: Laser tissue welding is a fantasy brought into reality by me in which tissues are held together by a glue instead of sutures which hardens after the application of laser light. It is described for the first time in medical literature by me. It has vast applications in pediatric surgery.
Materials and Methods: The three methods are as follows: (1) Glutaraldehyde Albumin Glue Enhanced Laser Tissue Welding-albumin (40%) mixed with 10% Glutaraldehyde in 10:1 proportion and applied over tissues and infrared Laser of 850 NM fired over it for 5 min makes a hard and waterproof bond. It requires an infrared laser costing Rs. 30 Lakhs which was indigenized by me in Rs. 10,000. In past 3 years, I performed 155 suture-less circumcisions with the technique. Technique was used to stop bleeding of factor 8 deficiency in three cases without factor 8 infusion, 7 cases of gall bladder fossa bleeding stopped with it. It was used to seal suture line of 108 cases of hypospadias, 13 cases of recto vestibular fistulae, 21 cases of bowel anastomoses, 7 cases of chledochoduodenostomy, 3 cases of TOF, 3 cases of grade 3 splenic injuries. 13 cases of hypospadias fistulae were treated which cured without surgery. (2) Chitosan Methylene blue film- One case of bowel perforation was sealed by Chitosan Methylene blue film fired by Laser successfully, reported for first time in medical literature. (3) Methylene blue dye enhanced laser tissue welding-Methylene blue infiltrated in tissues and Infrared laser now works on it as blue is opposite color. 21 cases of tongue tie released with it. 108 cases of hypospadias, 5 cases of splenic bleeding, 7 cases of liver tear, bleeding stopped which did not by electrocautery.
Results: Results were much better than control group with better outcome and less complications. Discussion: Glutaraldehyde Albumin Glue Enhanced Laser Tissue Welding forms a waterproof bond with great strength over tissues which is more than even sutures. It seals wound wonderfully so that dehiscence does not occur and makes suture line waterproof. Chitosan Methylene blue film merges with tissues after application of Laser and seals perforations without sutures which may be harmful in edematous tissues. Methylene blue dye enhanced laser tissue welding makes cheap infrared lasers work on tissues as blue color is opposite of infra-red. Bleeding that did not stop with electrocautery stops with this technique.
Conclusion: All 3 techniques of Laser tissue welding had great results compared to conventional techniques. We need more cases and longer follow up to develop it further.
ABSTRACT NO.: 235: Study to assess the effects of intraperitoneal retained cut suture ends in Wistar rats: Category: Experimental
MalviyaSonalParelkarSandeshSanghviBeejalGuptaRahulDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Seth G S and KEM Hospital, Mumbai, Maharashtra, India
E-mail: sonal.malviyaigmc@gmail.com
Introduction: Advanced minimal access surgery involves extensive intracorporeal suturing and results in lot of cut suture ends in operative field. The time and energy spent in retrieving these can be saved in case these cut ends can be left behind safely.
Aim: To assess the effects of intraperitoneal retained cut suture ends in Wistar rats.
Material and Method: This is an experimental model in which animals were subjected to laparotomy and cut suture ends are placed intraperitoneally. 18 adult Wistar rats were divided in three groups with 6 rats in each group. Suture used in Group 1- braided absorbable suture material (polyglycolic acid), Group 2- braided non absorbable suture material (polyester) and in Group 3- nonbraided non absorbable suture material (monofilament polyamide). Depending upon their group, suture material was introduced and left intraperitoneally under anaesthesia. The abdominal wall of rats in all groups was closed in a single layer with non-braided absorbable suture material (poliglecaprone 25). All animals were sacrificed after 4 weeks. The degree of inflammatory reaction in the peritoneal cavity was assessed.
Results: There were no morbidity or mortality. None of the rat developed feeding difficulties. The rate of adhesions found in the Group II (4/6) was significantly higher than the rate observed in the Group I (2/6) and Group III (0/6).
Conclusion: This pilot study concludes that depending on the type of suture materials used, braided suture materials preferably be removed. We can safely leave behind cut ends of suture materials intraperitoneally. This will help save time and efforts during advanced and prolonged laparoscopic surgeries. Further studies including study of histology to quantify are needed.
ABSTRACT NO.: 312: Role of preoperative nutritional status on operative outcome in children - prospective analysis of preoperative nutritional indices and micro nutrients affecting outcome in children undergoing surgery: Category: Experimental
VinayKGowrishankarJadhavVinayDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: vinaykonammek@gmail.com
Aim: Estimate the prevalence of malnutrition and micronutrient deficiency in children undergoing major surgery and outline the relationship between micronutrient deficiency and clinical outcomes. To correlate micronutrient deficiency, anthropometry, and conventional nutritional parameters to identify and define presurgical nutritional indicators predictive of nutrition-related complications.
Methods: Prospective observational cohort study done form January 2018 to January 2022 at a tertiary pediatric surgery center, funded by Rajiv Gandhi University. Children over the age of 3 months up to 17 years were included in this study. Only children planned for major elective surgery were recruited and children requiring emergency surgery, cancer-related surgery, and day care surgery were excluded from the study. Anthropometric details included weight, height, head circumference, mid-arm circumference, and triceps skin fold thickness (TSFT). At the time of surgery, blood samples were drawn and sent for analysis for albumin, prealbumin, transferrin, iron, Ferritin, B12, Zinc, folate, calcium, and Vitamin D. Events documented included duration, need for blood transfusion. In the postoperative period the need for ventilation, TPN, wound infection or any development of nutritional associated complications were documented with the time to start feeds and the length of hospital stay. Complications like wound infection, dehiscence, anastomotic leak, intestinal obstruction, redo surgery were documented during one month follow up. Descriptive data were reported as frequency and logistic regression model was used for correlation between variables.
Results: Of the total of 184 children evaluated stunting was seen in 73/176 children (39.2%) with varying level of micronutrient deficiency. There were a total of 20 (11%) incidents. 50 (28.2%) needed blood transfusion, 36 children developed wound infection (19.5%), redo surgery in the postoperative period was required in 16 (8.7%). Thirteen (7.1%) children required TPN while 4 (2.2%) required ventilators support. There was significant association of Zinc with wound related events; prealbumin and folate with need for transfusion; serum albumin with redo surgery. Among anthropometry only TSFT was related to LOS and stunting had no impact on outcome.
Conclusion: Ours is first study looking into the role of nutrition and micronutrients in the postoperative outcome in children. Micronutrient like zinc and folate with nutritional markers Albumin and prealbumin were significantly associated with postoperative incidents.
ABSTRACT NO.: 18: Human amniotic membrane usage from bench to bedside for congenital defects and wounds in pediatric surgery: A review: Category: Experimental
SinghSunitaAhmedIntezarSrivastavaNirajDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Raebareli, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: drsunitasingh28@gmail.com
Background: The restricted donor area in pediatric patients demands the use of human amniotic membrane (hAM) in difficult to manage wounds. It can be used directly over wounds or used to grow stem cells by different culture methods. The hAM can be used “fresh” i.e. +4O glycerol preserved hAM or “cryopreserved.”
Methods: The literature review was done up to April 2022 for all experimental models, randomized controlled trials (RCT), Observational studies, case series, and case reports involving the usage of hAM in the treatment of pediatric (>14 years.). The search included “PubMed,” “Web of Science” and “SCOPUS” with the keywords, “amnion,” “biomaterials,” “biological dressing,” “clinical study,” “congenital defects,” “human amniotic membrane,” “pediatric wound,” “neonatal wounds,” “regenerative medicine.” The search was extended by snowballing the reference list of all included studies.
Results: One experimental RCT, three review articles, and twelve case reports were included in final analysis. The experimental studies were in rat and porcine models. The pediatric second-and third-degree thermal burn followed by pediatric ocular diseases viz corneal epithelial ulcers, conjunctiva reconstruction following Steven−Johnson Syndrome and scarring after surgery of strabismus were the most common indications for usage of AM. Five cases of meningomyelocele repair (intradural and extradural placement of AM) and two cases of gastroschisis repair (as antiadhesive layer) were reported. Freeze-dried hAM is most frequently used in the clinical practice. Autologous hAM was used in antenatally detected birth defects. In the adults, the fresh hAM was found equally effective as freeze-dried AM. However, literature on the same is deficient in pediatric patients.
Conclusion: The hAM as a skin substitute in the pediatric wounds/defects showed an enhanced rate of healing. Further studies regarding the utility of “fresh or Freeze-dried AM in the management of pediatric wounds/anatomical defects,” can alleviate the economic constraints in the management of large/chronic wounds in developing countries.
ABSTRACT NO.: 230: Plasma GDNF levels in spina bifida: Correlation with neurological impairment in children: Category: Experimental
JosephDelona TreesaBajpaiMinuYadavDKSharmaSAnandSKhanMADepartment of Pediatric Surgery, AIIMS, New Delhi, India
E-mail: delonatreeza@gmail.com
Background: GDNF plays a crucial role in the stimulation of recovery, neuroplasticity, and synaptic reorganization after SCI providing neuroprotection and neuroregeneration. Plasma GDNF levels are upregulated in cases of spina bifida.
Objectives: To compare the plasma GDNF levels in cases of spina bifida with healthy controls and correlate with neurological impairment at 1 year of follow up.
Methods: Single center prospective analysis of cases of spina bifida at presentation and after 1 year of follow-up postsurgery. Controls were taken from the cases of hernia and hydrocele. Plasma GDNF levels were assessed.
Results: Eighty-five cases were included in the study. GDNF levels were elevated in cases compared to controls (mean 6.62 vs. 1.76) with significant P value (<0.01). Same was observed for open and closed defects (mean 7.6 vs. 4.86: P < 0.01). GDNF levels showed significant difference in cases with motor impairment, patulous sphincter, absent reflexes, bladder symptoms, radiological findings of AC type II, hydrocephalus, split cord malformation, and syrinx compared with those who did not (P < 0.01) at presentation. At 1 year of follow-up of 52 cases postsurgery ones with neurogenic bladder with abnormal UDS findings, requiring enema program, motor and sensory impairment, and hydrocephalus had significantly elevated baseline levels of GDNF compared with those who did not have these neurological impairment (P < 0.01).
Conclusion: There is significant elevation of plasma GDNF levels in cases of spina bifida and this elevation is proportional to the degree of spinal damage, and hence, the neurological impairment. GDNF levels are a good predictor for the severity of the lesion and thus the outcome in these cases.
ABSTRACT NO.: 253: Biomarker profile in children with bladder exstrophy under follow-up: A prospective study: Category: Experimental
JadhavShishirBajpaiMinuAnandSachitYadavDKVermaAjayDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, AIIMS, New Delhi, India
E-mail: jadhavshishir03@gmail.com
Aim: To study the role of urinary peptides trefoil factor family proteins (TFF3), neutrophil gelatinase-associated lipocalin (NGAL), and microalbuminuria (MLAB) as early markers of renal damage in patients of bladder exstrophy (BE) and to evaluate their association with conventional markers of progressive renal deterioration - Serum creatinine, glomerular-filtration rate(GFR), and Tc-99m dimercaptosuccinic acid (DMSA) scan.
Materials and Methods: The study included 50 consecutive children, aged <14 years, with BE and 35 age-matched controls. Examination of three urinary biomarkers, TFF 3, NGAL, and MALB was done, and kidney function was assessed by conventional markers of progressive renal deterioration - Serum creatinine, GFR, and DMSA scans at a given point in time during the follow-up of patients with BE. Progressive deterioration in the kidney function was defined as a fall in the GFR from ≥60 to <60 ml/min/1.73 m2 on comparing the baseline and latest DTPA scan. Groups 1 and 2 included children without and with progressive functional deterioration, respectively.
Results: The median age of children with BE and controls was 3 and 2.3 years, respectively. Median concentrations of TFF3, NGAL, and MLAB in patients were significantly elevated as compared to controls. Children belonging to Group 2 had significantly higher concentration of biomarkers as compared to those in group 1.
Conclusions: Urinary TFF 3, NGAL, and microalbumin significantly correlate with progressive deterioration of kidney function in children harboring BE. TFF3 showing strongest prediction of functional deterioration is an emerging biomarker to be included in the panel.
POSTER: Utility of laboratory markers in the diagnosis and prediction of perforation in acute appendicitis in children: A systematic review and meta-analysis: Category: GIT
KrishnanNellaiJukicMiroKrizanacZvonimirMunozCarlos Martin LlorentePogorelicZenonAnandSachitBajpaiMinuDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, New Delhi, India
E-mail: nellai93@gmail.com
Aims: The aim was to systematically summarize the utility of red cell distribution width (RDW), mean platelet volume (MPV) in the diagnosis of acute appendicitis (AA), and serum sodium levels in the prediction of perforation in AA.
Methods: Four scientific databases (PubMed, Scopus, Web of Science, and EMBASE) were systematically searched for relevant comparative studies by two independent researchers using appropriate keywords. The inclusion criteria were all comparative studies of MPV, RDW, or serum sodium in AA in children aged <18 years. The control group included either healthy controls or children with nonspecific abdomen pain (NSAP). The methodological quality was independently assessed by two authors using the Downs and Black scale.
Results: Pooling the data of RDW using a random effects model demonstrated no significant difference between children with AA and NSAP (weighted mean difference (WMD) = 0.99, 95% CI = [−0.35, 2.33], and P = 0.15). The pooling of the data of MPV values showed no statistically significant difference between AA and healthy controls (WMD) = −0.42, 95% CI= (−1.04, 0.20), P = 0.19) as well as between AA and NSAP (WMD = 0.01, 95% CI= [−0.15, 0.17], P = 0.90). Pooling the data for serum sodium levels demonstrated significantly lower serum sodium levels in children with complicated AA as compared to uncomplicated AA (WMD: −3.18, 95% CI −4.67–−1.69, P < 0.0001).
Conclusion: There is no evidence of the utility of RDW and MPV values in the diagnostic testing of AA in children. Low-serum sodium levels in children can predict complicated AA.
Malrotation – Age no bar: Category: GIT
TotadriVarsha MAcharyaSamir KantPuriArchanaSugandhiNidhiJadhavAmitTeckchandaniNarinderSreedharanAnjanaSinghGauravVijiGThakurSonaliniBeheraVikramChakrabortyAnwesaKanodiaShrishtiVinodY V SShelkeVinayakumarVardhman Mahavir Medical College and Safdarjung Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: varsha2892@gmail.com
Aim: The aim is to evaluate the spectrum of presentation of malrotation among various pediatric age groups.
Materials and Methods: A retrospective analysis of all cases of malrotation who presented to our center, between 2018 and 2022.
Results: Forty-eight patients (38 males, 10 females) were diagnosed with malrotation and underwent surgical intervention-Ladd procedure. Fifteen (31.2%) were more than 2 years of age. Twenty-two (45.8%) were neonates. The youngest patient was 1 day of life and the oldest was 12 years of age. The mean time of starting feeds was on 4th postobstructive diuresis. The mean duration of stay was 6.5 days. Thirty-seven of 48 patients (77.08%) had associated midgut volvulus. Five had associated gangrene of the bowel (10.4%). Three of 48 patients (all above 5 years of age) had associated paraduodenal hernia. One child presented at 5 months of age, having been operated on day 5 of life for left congenital diaphragmatic hernia (CDH), and was diagnosed here as recurrent left CDH-intraoperative findings showed malrotation. No associated gastrointestinal anomalies were noted in any of the patients. Eleven of 15 patients above 2 years of age (73%) presented with chronic symptoms, requiring contrast-enhanced computed tomography abdomen or barium meal follow through (BMFT) to confirm the diagnosis. Four patients (8.3%) underwent resection of bowel + stoma formation. 4 of 48 (8.3%) patients expired postsurgically (all were neonates).
Conclusion: Malrotation can have an acute presentation or manifest with chronic symptoms. Both presentations vary in the diagnostic evaluation but ultimately, prompt identification and immediate surgery are the primary modality of treatment to prevent the hazardous complication of bowel gangrene.
Case of delayed splenic rupture in case of splenic laceration following blunt trauma abdomen: Category: GIT
PillaiAiswarya RAravindC SKumarG M AsokSethunathSBeenaS VDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Government Medical College, Thiruvanathapuram, Kerala, India
E-mail: aiswarya.kairali@gmail.com
Aim: The aim is to highlight the delayed presentation of splenic injury and to discuss the management options.
Methods: Data were collected from Hospital records and discharged summary.
Results: Blunt trauma abdomen is a frequent emergency and is associated with high morbidity and mortality. The spleen is the most common organ involved. Seventy percent can be managed conservatively with a failure rate of 2%–3%. This poster involves a case of blunt trauma abdomen with splenic injury managed conservatively presented 1 month later with a hemodynamically unstable delayed splenic rupture. The case of an 11-year-old female with a history of fall from cycle presented with hemoperitoneum. At the time of presentation vitals were stable no pallor. Computed tomography (CT) abdomen showed splenic laceration Grade III. Her serial HB monitoring showed fall in hemoglobin and hence blood transfusion was given. She was tested positive for COVID-19 but was asymptomatic. She was managed conservatively with nil per oral (NPO), antibiotic, and other supportive measures. Before discharge ultrasound (USS) abdomen was done and no hemoperitoneum and spleen was reported as normal. She was discharged and post discharge USS at 2 weeks was a normal study. Hence he was discharged but 1-month post discharge she presented with syncope and pallor. Urgent resuscitation was started. She was admitted to the pediatric intensive care unit. CT angiogram was done and showed pseudoaneurysm of the splenic artery. She underwent laparotomy + splenectomy. Intraoperatively, she had massive hemoperitoneum and was found to have a splenic rupture. Splenectomy was done. There was no other solid or hollow viscus injury. She took all the postsplenectomy vaccines and was discharged on POD10.
Conclusions: Hemodynamically, stable patients who are managed conservatively should be kept in close follow-up as there is a risk of delayed rebleed.
Cervical esophagostomy and feeding abdominal esophagostomy followed by retrosternal gastric pull-up is a feasible method in managing long-gap esophageal atresias: Category: GIT
DesaiDhaneshDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, LYDM Sparsh Hospital, Gadag-Betageri, Karnataka, India
E-mail: drdhaneshdesai@gmail.com
Aims: For long-gap esophageal atresia (pure esophageal atresia) and in case of a major anastomotic leak following primary repair, cervical esophagostomy and feeding abdominal esophagostomy (surgery 1) followed by retrosternal gastric pull-up surgery (surgery 2) is a feasible and relatively easy procedure.
Methods: This is a prospective study of four patients operated by a single surgeon followed up from august 2016 to July 2022. Three cases of pure esophageal atresia and one case of major leak after primary repair were included. Intraoperative challenges and postoperative follow-up of average 13 months after surgery 1 and intraoperative challenges and average postoperative follow-up of 39 months are documented. Various parameters such as stomal regression, stricture, peristomal excoriation, ease of recannulation, and read mission were studied after surgery 1. Immediate postoperative complications such as anastomotic leak, feeding problems, and hospital stay and later follow-up in the form of weight gain, feeding difficulties, and readmissions were studied after surgery 2.
Results: Surgery 1 – srimal stricture and regression were not seen in all the patients. Peristomal excoriation was seen in one patient. The ease of re-cannulation was excellent. The readmission rate was minimal. Surgery 2 – intraoperative mobilization, creating retrosternal space, and ease of pull-up were good and encouraging. The immediate postoperative period was satisfying. Delayed problems were less and satisfactory weight gain and less readmissions were noticed.
Conclusion: Cervical esophagostomy and feeding abdominal esophagostomy followed by retrosternal gastric pull-up surgery is a good alternative in managing long-gap esophageal atresia and failed primary anastomoses.
One stage transanal pull through for Hirschsprung's disease in our institutes – A case series: Category: GIT
YuvarajMSundariS MeenakshiKaruppasamyNAravindhanCSrinivasakumarRDevSathiaDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Madurai Medical College, Madurai, Tamil Nadu, India
E-mail: yuvikapv@gmail.com
Aim: The aim of study is to share our institutional experience in transanal pullthrough procedure. Surgical management of Hirschsprung's disease has evolved from original 3 stage procedure to a single-stage one. We analyzed the result of one stage transanal pull-through for Hirschsprung's disease in our institute.
Methods: In 2 years, seven cases were presented in various age groups. They all underwent transanal pull-through procedure. All children were evaluated with barium enema. Children with classical Hirschprung picture only were included in this study. Others were excluded from the study.
Results: The mean age of surgery was 1 year 80% of children underwent surgery within meantime. The mean time to start oral feeding was 1 day. The most common clinical presentation in the above-said children was constipation or enterocolitis. All children were discharged with no major postoperative complications.
Discussion: Transanal pull-through is essentially the same surgery that has been performed open for decades but it avoids the need for a laparotomy and intra-abdominal mobilization of rectum the lack of intra-abdominal dissection should result in shorter time to feeds and to discharge and possibly a lower incidence of intra-abdominal adhesions.
Pancreatic pseudocyst in pediatric patients – Etiology, presentation, and management: Category: GIT
KanodiaShrishtiShelkeVinayakumarVinodVenkata SaiTotadriVarsha MJadhavAmitAcharyaSamir KPuriArchanaSugandhiNidhiDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Vardhaman Mahavir Medical College and Safdarjung Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: shrishti008@gmail.com
Introduction: Pancreatic pseudocysts are rare in children. There is very less information regarding causes, optimal management (external vs. internal drainage), and long-term outcomes of pseudocysts in children.
Aim: This study aimed to analyze the causes, clinical presentation, management, and outcomes of pancreatic pseudocysts treated at our institution.
Methods: This was a retrospective study including all children diagnosed with pancreatic pseudocyst from 2019 to 2022. The collected data included age, sex, etiology, clinical presentation, radiological information, management, complications, and the final outcome.
Results: A total of 15 patients (11 females, 4 males (M:F-2.75:1) were included. Trauma was the most common antecedent cause 11/15 (73%), followed by idiopathic chronic pancreatitis 2/15 (13.3%) and choledochal cyst-associated pancreatitis in 2/15 (13.3%). The most common presenting symptom was pain 86%, abdominal mass 26.6%, and vomiting 13.3%. The duration of symptoms at presentation ranged from 15 days to 2 months. All could be diagnosed conclusively with an ultrasonogram (USG) and contrast-enhanced computed tomography abdomen. Six (40%) underwent external drainage in the form of USG-guided pig tail catheter insertion, 2 (13.3%) underwent cystogastrostomy and 7 (4.6%) were managed conservatively. Monitoring was done on the basis of repeated USG and clinical improvement. There was recurrence seen in 2/15 (13.3%) after external drainage, which resolved on conservative management.
Conclusion: Although surgical management of pseudocysts in the form of internal drainage or excision was preferred in the past, this review shows that in children external drainage and conservative management of pseudocysts results in good symptomatic relief, minimal morbidity, and recurrences.
Pediatric small bowel obstruction – A case series of rare causes of internal herniation: Category: GIT
PaiNitin GVenkatramananAarthiSundeepP TKumarVijayPrabhuSantoshRbnRanjaniDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Kasturba Medical College, MAHE, Manipal, Karnataka, India
E-mail: nitingpai@gmail.com
Aims: Internal hernias as a cause of bowel obstruction can be defined as herniation of viscera into an opening within the peritoneal cavity. The most common internal hernias to become symptomatic in childhood are paraduodenal and transmesenteric. The purpose of this case series is to highlight the possibility of these rare diagnoses in pediatric small bowel obstruction.
Methods: A retrospective analysis of four pediatric cases who present with acute small bowel obstruction due to internal herniation.
Results: Case 1: A 2-year-old female child with acute intestinal obstruction due to the left paraduodenal hernia with associated malrotation. Case 2: A 16-year-old with a history of the right solitary kidney with ureteric reimplantation presents with acute intestinal obstruction due to malrotation with the right para duodenal hernia. Case 3: A 6-year-old male with acute intestinal obstruction due to broad-based Meckel's diverticulum with a band. Case 4: A 5-year-old male with acute intestinal obstruction gangrenous ileum due to internal herniation underwent resection and ileostomy given a septic shock.
Conclusion: Internal hernias are rare, having an autopsy incidence of <1% in the general population. Para duodenal hernias are further subdivided based on their anatomical site and embryological origin, as left (from the Landzert's fossa) and right (from the Waldeyer's fossa-rare). The best imaging modality to utilize when considering a diagnosis of congenital internal hernia is the upper GI contrast study. A high index of suspicion and urgent surgical exploration by laparotomy/laparoscopy usually end up with good outcomes.
Total urogenital mobilization in common cloaca: Our experience six cases: Category: GIT
SharmaShyamendra PratapChowdharySaritaPanigrahiPranaya KumarKumarDeepakSharmaShiv PrasadDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, IMS, BHU, Varanasi, Uttar Pradesh, India
E-mail: shyambrd07@gmail.com
Aim: The aim is to present our experience in surgical management of short common channel common cloaca patients.
Methods: This retrospective study included 6 children who were diagnosed and treated short common channel (<3 cm) common cloaca from January 2017 to January 2020 in pediatric surgery department of IMS BHU. Patient's data of 3 years were retrieved from case sheets and analyzed. Ultrasonography and magnetic resonance imaging pelvis were used for imaging purpose. Pan-endoscopy was done in all patients. Patients with long common channel (>5 cm) and lost to follow-up were excluded.
Results: Six patients were included in the study. Age ranged from 6 months to 7 years. Bowel diversion was done as transverse colostomy in four patients, high sigmoid colostomy in two patients had tube vaginostomy had been done in two patients due to hydrocolpos in the neonatal period. Imaging revealed associated anomalies as sacral anomalies in two, genital anomalies in one, ectopic kidney one patient. The length of the common channel was <3 cm in all patients (1.5–3 cm) confirmed during pan-endoscopy. Total urogenital mobilization (TUM) with posterior sagittal ano-recto-urethro-vaginoplasty (PSARUVP) was done in all cases. Postoperative complications were found in 2 patients as wound dehiscence, catheter dislodgement. Follow-up was done from 1 year to 3 years.
Conclusion: The short common channel common cloaca patients can be fairly managed through TUM with PSARUVP. Continuous long-term follow-up is required to determine the long-term results of corrective surgery.
Pediatric Gastrointestinal Basidiobolomycosis – The surgical considerations in the youngest patient reported from India: Category: GIT
VigAyushiSaxenaRahulSharmaAnuradhaYadavTarunaSinhaArvindDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, All India Institute of Medical Sciences, Jodhpur, Rajasthan, India
E-mail: ayushivig@gmail.com
Background: Gastrointestinal basidiobolomycosis is a rare but challenging disease. It can affect all age groups, however, slight predominance is noted in children. Diagnosis is crucial owing to the rarity of the disease. Although monotherapy with azoles is a treatment with favorable outcomes, surgical intervention becomes imperative in a few difficult cases. Here, we report the complex multi-staged surgical reconstruction which was required in the youngest reported patient of gastrointestinal basidiobolomycosis.
Materials: A 2-month-old male baby belonging to the arid state of Rajasthan in the Indian subcontinent presented to the hospital emergency with intestinal obstruction and underwent ileostomy and biopsy of the abdominal mass. The diagnosis was established on the basis of the microscopic examination of the pyogenic material and culture in Sabouraud's dextrose agar. Oral itraconazole was continued for 36 months before the complete disappearance of the fungal mass in imaging.
Results: During the disease, the patient developed right ureteric stricture and sigmoid stricture for which he underwent a successful ureteric stricture resection and anastomosis and an unsuccessful sigmoid stricture resection. The sigmoid stricture was later dealt with laparoscopic-assisted transanal endorectal pull-through and subsequently, the ileostomy was closed. The patient is healthy and thriving well after 1 year of follow-up.

Conclusion:

Gastrointestinal basidiobolomycosis is a rare disease with varying presentations such as the mass abdomen, bowel disturbances, liver mass, and intestinal obstruction
Long-term azole therapy with serial monitoring of liver, renal, and cardiovascular function is the treatment of choice
Structures engulfed in the mass heal with stricture formation. This case represents how this disease can pose a surgical challenge even in the recovery phase, requiring multiple surgical interventions.

Pediatric surgeon and encephalitis – An unusual combination: Category: GIT
ManasaABabuNarendraDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Indira Gandhi Institute of Child Health, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: manasa.ananda@gmail.com
Background: Twelve-year-old female who presented with neuropsychiatric symptoms had a hidden pathology. We present this case report owing to the rarity and surprises in the diagnosis.
Case Report: A 12-year-old prepubertal female presented with complaints of fever for 3 days, followed by the development of altered sensorium, irrelevant speech, uncontrollable laughter, and apparent loss of memory. She developed four episodes of generalized tonic–clonic seizure, at a local hospital. Cerebrospinal fluid analysis revealed anti-N-methyl-D-aspartate (NMDA)-Receptor antibodies. Antiepileptics and intravenous immunoglobulin (IVIG) started and referred to our hospital. Glasgow Coma scale: E2V2M4. The tone was increased. She started intravenous steroids, rituximab, and risperidone and continued with IVIG and antiepileptics.
USG Abdomen: Bulky right ovary with a cystic lesion −4.1 cm × 1.8 cm, hypoechoic mural nodule, and calcific foci noted within. Magnetic resonance imaging pelvis-Enlarged right ovary with cystic and fatty areas – suggestive of right ovarian teratoma. The left ovary-poly cystic appearance. Three such cases are reported in the pediatric age group, none in the prepubertal age. Antibodies are generated in response to the neural elements in the teratoma which react with the NMDA-1 receptor expressed in the forebrain and hippocampus-implicated in memory and learning. The child was optimized and underwent laparoscopic right ovary-sparing teratoma excision with drilling of the left ovarian cysts. The child showed dramatic improvement with respect to her sensorium, memory, and speech.
HPE: Gross: 2 cm × 2 cm × 1.2 cm capsulated mass, microscopy: Suggestive of mature cystic teratoma with no immature neuroepithelium. She was discharged on a tapering dose of steroids, phenytoin, and risperidone. Following completion of rituximab course (4 doses), she was advised to review with CD 20 counts.
Conclusion: Anti-NMDA encephalitis is a very rare disorder. This is probably the first case to be reported with a mature teratoma. A high index of suspicion and appropriate imaging is the cornerstone of diagnosis. The management being straightforward ensures complete recovery.
Advocating for neonatal hernia repair as an emergency: Category: GIT
DudhaniShreyasJanaSouravSinhaAmit KumarKumarBindeyDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, AIIMS, Patna, Bihar, India
E-mail: shreyas.dudhani45@gmail.com
Background (Aim): Inguinal hernias are more common in preterm and neonates and incidence of incarceration is reported to be more in the first 6 months of life. Strangulation follows incarceration and various incarcerated and strangulated contents having been reported in the sac. The fistulation of the herniated content through the scrotal skin is quite rare with only a few reported cases. We present a case of enteroscrotal fistula in a neonate managed with staged repair along with a brief review of literature.
Case (Methods + Results): A 27-day-old full-term male presented with acute onset scrotal swelling with fecal discharge from the right scrotum for 1 day. At presentation, he was hemodynamically stable with no tell-tale signs of obstructed hernia. A 2 cm × 1 cm fecal fistulous opening was located in the right hemiscrotum. The terminal 8 cm of ileum was seen as the content with an antimesenteric perforation. Divided ileostomy and scrotal debridement were done. He was discharged uneventfully and doing well at 3-month follow-up. Six months after 1st surgery, ileo-ascending anastomosis was done electively. The patient is clinically healthy now.
Conclusions: Neonatal hernias should be considered an emergency and we advocate early surgery. We feel such cases have been under-reported and the prognosis which has generally been reported as good may not be the actual scenario. In our case, swelling and fistulation occurred in a very brief period of 36 h. We managed the child with a diversion stoma followed by ileoascending anastomosis later.
Achalasia cardia in children: A retrospective study: Category: GIT
SayeedAdnanKumarNitinAlladiAnandDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Bangalore Medical College and Research Institute, Bengaluru, Karnataka, India
E-mail: adnansayeed1711@gmail.com
Introduction: Achalasia cardia is a neuromuscular disorder of unknown etiology characterized by aperistalsis of the body of the esophagus and failure of relaxation of the lower esophageal sphincter. The diagnosis of achalasia cardia is delayed because of the rarity and the ability to mimic other common conditions in children. Hence, a study was conducted to evaluate the clinical presentation and the management of achalasia cardia in children.
Methods: A retrospective observational study was conducted in the department of pediatric surgery at a tertiary center. The children with achalasia cardia presented between January 2014 and December 2021 were included.
Results: A total of 12 patients were treated for achalasia cardia during the study. The duration of symptoms varied from 2 months to 11 years with a median of 1.25 years. All children presented with recurrent episodes of vomiting while dysphagia was seen in six (50%) children. Eighty-three percent of the children presented with a history of weight loss and failure to thrive (weight-for-age <3rd centile) was seen in nine (75%) children. Five (42%) children were managed with gastroesophageal reflux disease (GERD) for more than a year before presenting to us. Three (25%) children underwent open cardiomyotomy with Thal fundoplication and the rest nine (75%) underwent laparoscopic cardiomyotomy. None of our patients had perforation or stricture. Two (22%) of our patients had a recurrence of symptoms managed by dilatations. All are thriving well.
Conclusion: Achalasia is an important differential diagnosis in children with suspected GERD. The most common symptom of achalasia cardia is vomiting followed by dysphagia, unlike in adults. Weight loss and failure to thrive are important presenting features in children with achalasia which are uncommon in adults. Laparoscopic cardiomyotomy is safe and effective to treat achalasia cardia in children.
Keywords: Achalasia cardia, cardiomyotomy, fundoplication, gastroesophageal reflux disease
A rare case of true pancreatic cyst lesion in a child: Category: GIT
SushmaASharmaRahul DeoSinghSurendraRedkarRajeevLilavati Hospital and Research Centre, Mumbai, Maharashtra, India
E-mail: sushmaachugatla10@gmail.com
Background: Pancreatic cysts can be true or pseudocysts. Pseudocysts are relatively more common in children than true pancreatic cysts. Here in, we report a case of true pancreatic cyst in the pediatric age group.
Case Report: A 2-year-old boy presented with pain in the abdomen and high-grade fever for 7 days. On examination, he was tachycardic and tachypnoeic. He had the fullness of periumbilical and left lumbar regions. A soft cystic mass was felt in the umbilical and left lumbar region, with tenderness over the mass. Ultrasonography showed a well-defined anechoic cystic mass of 9 cm × 8 cm × 6 cm, arising from the lower aspect of body and tail of pancreas with thick wall in left hypochondrium, left lumbar and extending to left iliac fossa. A computed tomography abdomen suggested a possibility of duplication cyst of the intestine or pseudocyst of the pancreas. Blood tests were normal with raised serum amylase and lipase levels. Supportive management was given. Magnetic resonance imaging showed a possibility of intestinal duplication cyst or pseudocyst of the pancreas. Percutaneous decompression with pigtail catheter insertion was carried out. Due to catheter displacement as an unforeseen event, we went ahead with laparotomy, on which giant pancreatic cyst was found, adherent to the transverse colon near the splenic flexure. Cyst was marsupialized with under-running of the cyst wall. Postoperatively, recovery was smooth and was discharged with a check sonography confirming no residual cystic collection. Histopathological diagnosis was confirmed as true pancreatic cyst. Follow-up was uneventful.
Conclusion: Although rare, a true pancreatic cyst should be considered in the differential diagnosis of a child of <2 years with cystic lesions of the abdomen.

SinghSurendraSharmaRahul DeoAchugatlaSushmaKarmarkarSantoshLilavati Hospital and Research Centre, Mumbai, Maharashtra, India
E-mail: drsurendrasinghkgmu@gmail.com
Introduction: Intractable constipation is an impairing problem commonly associated with anorectal malformations (ARM), Hirschsprung's disease, and Spina bifida. We present a case where left-sided ACE procedure was done using the ascending colon in a case of ARM, HSCR with malrotation.
Case Report: A 15-year-old female presented with complaints of persistent chronic constipation. She was a known case of operated HSCR, malrotation, and ARM. At 4 years of age, she was diagnosed with Hirschsprung's disease on a proven rectal biopsy. For which, she underwent Swenson's pull-through. Intraoperatively, malrotation was detected and was corrected along with appendicectomy. As she continued to have persistent constipation, left-sided ACE with antireflux mechanism was performed. Postprocedure, the patient is passing stools regularly on an antegrade enema.
Conclusion: The left colon ACE using a colonic tube with antireflux mechanism is a convenient operation for chronic constipation. This is a rare case of operated ARM, HSCR, and malrotation in which colon was on the left side and appendicectomy had been done as a part of malrotation correction. Hence, a left-sided ACE was performed in this case.

Surgically correctable central cyanosis: Congenital Extrahepatic portosystemic shunt in a child: Category: GIT
BadeRamyasreePetersNitin JamesKiranKhyatiSenInduMenonPremaSamujhRamVaidyaPankajDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, PGIMER, Chandigarh, India
E-mail: ramyabade@gmail.com
Aim: Congenital extrahepatic portosystemic shunts (CEPS) is characterized by the abnormal congenital connection between the portal system proximal to its bifurcation with the supra renal inferior vena cava. They are a rare cause of Hepatopulmonary syndrome. The presentation and management are variables based on the type of shunt, age, and presentation. The diagnostic dilemma, surgical management, and outcome in one such case are discussed.
Methods: Our index case is a 10-year-old boy with a 5-year history of central cyanosis, Grade 3 pandigital clubbing, and dyspnea on exertion. The saturation on room air was 65%–70% and postoxygenation was 97%. With the possibility of interstitial lung disease versus cardiac versus extra cardiac shunting, chest X-ray, bronchoalveolar lavage, pulmonary function tests, and angiogram of chest were done, which were normal. A bubble echocardiogram revealed extra cardiac shunting. Technetium perfusion scan revealed a 50.1% lung shunt fraction; however, the site of shunting could not be localized. Abdominal angiography showed Type II CEPS between the inferior mesenteric vein and the right internal iliac vein and clinched the diagnosis in our case. Percutaneous transcatheter occlusion of the shunt was not feasible. Single-stage surgical ligation was done after the occlusion test. Postoperatively, the child had hospital-acquired pneumonia and partial portal vein thrombosis which improved gradually. Repeat lung scintiscan showed a reduction in lung shunt fraction to 40%.
Results: The postoperative saturation on room air was around 85%–87% along with a significant symptomatic improvement in dyspnea. The child is currently doing well and is on regular follow-up.
Conclusion: CEPS should be considered in children presenting with dyspnea and hypoxia after ruling out cardiac and respiratory causes. With early intervention, children can have drastic improvement in symptoms. There is a need to develop a robust anticoagulation and follow-up protocol for this often-undiagnosed condition.
Long-tailed gastric trichobezoar – A rare cause of recurrent small bowel intussusceptions: Category: GIT
ShelkeVinayakumarVinodY SShrishtiKVarshaTNidhiSSamirkantAArchanaPNarenderDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Safdarjung hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: kumarvinaya1993@gmail.com
A rare case of long-tailed trichobezoar leading to recurrent small gut intussusception is reported.
Methods: A 6-year-old boy has presented to the ER with acute abdominal pain and nonbilious vomiting. The child has history of previous 3 admissions over 4 months, for small bowel intussusception which was managed conservatively.
At the present admission, he was found to have a palpable epigastric mass as well. The ultrasonography again revealed multiple small bowel intussusceptions. A contrast-enhanced computed tomography showed a nonhomogeneous circumscribed attenuated mass with a mottled gas appearance in the lumen of the stomach which was extending till proximal jejunum, subjective of a trichobezoar.
There was no history of psychological disorders, developmental issues, or trichotillomania.
Results: Anterior gastrotomy through a horizontal incision over the body of the stomach revealed a giant trichobezoar of size 8 cm × 7 cm × 6 cm with a very large tail (more than 60 cm in size). No additional enterotomy was required and the presence of additional daughter trichobezoars was ruled out. Multiple small bowel intussusceptions (4 no) manually reduced with no evidence of lead point.
Conclusion: Recurrent intussusception cases should be evaluated thoroughly for any lead points however a trichobezoar with a long tail is out of area of clinical suspicion. Surgical exploration and complete removal of the trichobezoar or the lead point is the mainstay of treatment. A long-tailed trichobezoar leading to pathological small bowel intussusception is hitherto reported.
Mesenteric Meckel's diverticulum leading to intussusception: Expect the unexpected: Category: GIT
ShelkeVinayakumarVinodY SShrishtiKVarshaTNidhiSSamirkantAArchanaPNarenderAmitJAnjanaGauravSDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, Safdarjung hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: kumarvinaya1993@gmail.com
Introduction: Meckel's diverticulum is the most common congenital anomaly of the small intestine, affecting 1%–4% of the population. Cardinal features emphasize an antimesenteric location. A brain teasing case with small bowel intussusception secondary to the Meckel's arising from the mesenteric border of the bowel is reported here.
Case Report: This report examines the case of a 6-year-old male child presenting with a 4-month history of episodic central abdominal pain, nonbilious vomiting, on admission ultrasound suggestive of small bowel intussusception and spontaneously resolved after 12 h, and also CT as initial CT findings were inconclusive.
The patient presented with similar complaints with ultrasonography findings suggestive of small bowel intussusception, taken up for emergency surgery, Intraoperatively, he was found to have small bowel intussusception approximately 40 cm proximal to the ileocaecal valve. The resected small bowel segment on examination revealed a mesenteric outpouching that was confirmed as mesenteric MD on histopathological analysis. Postoperatively, the patient recovered with no surgical complications and full symptom resolution.
Conclusion: Meckel's diverticulum has been a well-established cause of intussusception. However, a case with Meckel's arising from the antemesentric border leading to an intussusception is rarely reported in the existing literature.
Pediatric appendicitis risk calculator – A novel clinical tool for diagnosis of acute appendicitis: Category: GIT
GautamVasuRashmiDKhannaChetnaPatelRishabhMishalSahilDebnathPinaki RanjanMeenaAtul KKundalVijayShahShaluSenAmitaDepartment of Pediatric Surgery, ABVIMS and Dr RML Hospital, New Delhi, India
E-mail: vasugautam91@gmail.com
Introduction: Acute appendicitis is one of the most frequent causes of emergency abdominal surgery in childhood, posing a large burden on health-care system. We are evaluating a new score, i.e., pediatric Appendicitis Risk Calculator, recently derived and validated in the western population, and determine its utility for the diagnosis of appendicitis in the Indian pediatric population.
Objective: The objective is to evaluate Pediatric Appendicitis Risk Calculator as a complimentary tool for the diagnosis of acute appendicitis in children.
Materials and Methods: This study has been conducted in the Department of pediatric surgery, ABVIMS, and Dr. RML Hospital, New Delhi, from December 2019 to May 2022. A total of 96 cases aged 2–12 years were included, who presented to the outpatient department/emergency with clinical features of acute appendicitis with the onset of symptoms within 96 h and were subsequently taken up for appendectomy. Pediatric Appendicitis Risk Calculator (pARC) risk score was calculated for these patients in preoperative period and postoperatively histopathological examination was done to confirm appendicitis.
Results: All 96 cases were histopathologically proven cases of appendiceal inflammation in form of either acute appendicitis or appendicular perforation. pARC score showed 0 patient in ultra-low and low-risk groups, 3 in low-moderate risk group, 32 in moderate risk group, 46 in moderate high-risk group, and 15 in high-risk group. Our study shows that more than 95% of the patients operated for acute appendicitis that were also histologically proven to be a case of appendiceal inflammation lied in moderate, moderate high- and high-risk groups.
Conclusion: pARC is an effective and easy tool to guide the management of patients presenting with clinical features of acute appendicitis. It can easily be used by any medical personnel at triage to immediately refer moderate-high and high-risk cases for surgical intervention and patients with low risk can be observed in the emergency room or can be discharged.
